# P-FAST-PLATE mechanistic grounding report

---

## A. THE TASK

### Prompt format

**One-shot, direct, no CoT.** The scoring prompt is the constant string (defined at `writeback_compile.py` line 151):

```python
DIRECT_PROMPT = ("The {lm} is a famous landmark. The capital of the "
                 "country where it is located is")
```

The model is evaluated by **greedy first-token argmax** restricted to the union candidate set (capitals ∪ countries ∪ cities from BANK, clash-dropped). That is the entire inference; no chain-of-thought tokens are generated during scoring.

Three other prompt forms exist but are not scoring prompts:
- `TEACHER_PROMPT` (line 153): `"The {lm} is located in {c}. The capital of {c} is"` — used only as the GD teacher input.
- `COT_PROMPT` (line 155): used only during gate-0 to check host CoT competence.
- `G_QUERY_PREFIX + G_QUERY` (lines 158–161): used only for the g-hop accuracy measurement (landmark→country).

### The BANK and splits

Defined at `writeback_compile.py` lines 84–150. Structure: `BANK = {country: (capital, [(landmark, city, split), ...])}`.

- **16 countries total.** 8 TRAIN countries (Spain, India, Egypt, UAE, Morocco, Italy, Brazil, Turkey), 8 B2 countries (France, Germany, Canada, Australia, Switzerland, Poland, Vietnam, China).
- **TRAIN split**: 2 landmarks per TRAIN country. 16 total defined, but 15 valid after gate-0 (Blue Mosque fails CoT; see below).
- **B1 split (held-landmark)**: 2 more landmarks per TRAIN country, drawn from the same countries but not seen during any delta construction. 16 valid.
- **B2 split (held-country)**: 3 landmarks per B2 country. B2 countries are entirely absent from any arm's construction. 22 valid (St. Mary's Basilica fails g_ok; Golden Bridge fails g_ok).

Constraint: `∀cell: cell.city ≠ cell.capital` (shortcut-free; every landmark is in a non-capital city).

### What makes B2 the sharp "wire-vs-lookup" test

B2 countries are **structurally isolated**: no B2 landmark, city, or country appears in any arm's key construction or calibration. A construct/routing-write arm that only memorized TRAIN pairs (landmark→capital) would have no stored entry for B2 landmarks. The only way to score correctly on B2 is to have internalized the **compositional rule** (landmark→country key fires → writes capital of that country), where the country→capital mapping generalizes to unseen countries. The `construct_lookup` arm (landmark-keyed neurons, TRAIN only) is the explicit materialized-view null and **must fail B2 by construction**; G2 verifies `arm_B2 > lookup_B2`.

B1 tests held-landmark generalization within TRAIN countries, but cannot distinguish wire from lookup (a lookup could have stored country→capital for all 8 TRAIN countries). B2 adds the orthogonal constraint: held-country.

### Cell scoring

`eval_cells()` (`writeback_compile.py` line 723; `routing_register.py` line ~491 `eval_cells()`):

1. Run `logits_last(DIRECT_PROMPT.format(lm=c.landmark))` → logit vector over full vocabulary.
2. `argmax_union(lo)` = `max(union, key=lambda w: lo[union[w]])` — restricted to union candidate set token IDs.
3. `correct = float(first_word(arg) == first_word(c.capital))` — first-word match (accent-tolerant via `fold()`).

**Correct = 1.0 iff the argmax over union candidates is the capital's first token (accent-folded).** Margins and advisory generations are recorded but not gated.

### gate0.json — what it is and how it is produced

Gate-0 is computed **before any weight surgery**, during `run_model()` at `writeback_compile.py` lines 554–594. For each cell in BANK:

1. **g_ok**: `max(countries, key=lambda w: logits_last(G_QUERY_PREFIX + G_QUERY.format(lm=c.landmark))[first_tid(w)]) == c.country` — can the frozen host route the landmark to its country?
2. **h_ok**: `first_word(max(caps, key=...logits_last(CAP_PREFIX + CAP_QUERY.format(x=c.country))[first_tid(w)])) == first_word(c.capital)` — can the frozen host produce the capital given the country?
3. **cot_ok**: `contains(fold(cot_gen), fold(c.capital))` where `cot_gen = gen(COT_PROMPT.format(lm=c.landmark), COT_TOKENS)` — does the CoT generation contain the capital string?

A cell is **valid** iff g_ok AND h_ok AND cot_ok. The file is written to `{out}/gate0.json`. Gate-0 passes iff `all(ns[sp] >= MIN_PER_SPLIT=8 for sp in SPLITS)` and `cot_rate >= HOST_COT_FLOOR=0.70`.

### The ~53 valid cells (actual: exactly 53)

From the executed run (`results/writeback-compile/qwen3-4b/gate0.json`):
- **Total cells**: 56 (16 countries × 3–4 landmarks each).
- **Valid**: 53. Splits: TRAIN=15, B1=16, B2=22.
- **Dropped cells** (3):
  - *Blue Mosque* (TRAIN, Turkey): g_ok=True, h_ok=True, **cot_ok=False** — the model's CoT claimed Istanbul is the capital, not Ankara.
  - *St. Mary's Basilica* (B2, Poland): **g_ok=False**, h_ok=True — model failed to route to Poland.
  - *Golden Bridge* (B2, Vietnam): **g_ok=False**, h_ok=True — model failed to route to Vietnam.
- **union_dropped**: empty — no first-token clashes in the union candidate set.

`routing_register.py` **loads this frozen gate0.json** from the s303 record dir (`--record-dir`; default `results/writeback-compile/qwen3-4b`) rather than recomputing it (lines 297–311 of routing_register.py):

```python
g0 = json.loads((rec / "gate0.json").read_text())
valid = [wb.Cell(**{k: c[k] for k in fields}) for c in g0["cells"]
         if c.get("g_ok") and c.get("h_ok") and c.get("cot_ok")]
```

---

## B. THE MODEL SURGERY

### Model

**Qwen/Qwen3-4B** (`results.json: model_id`). `n_layers = 36`. `dec` = the list of decoder layers obtained via `mh3.resolve_parts(model)` (`operand_multihop3.resolve_parts`).

### Install band and install layer

- `INSTALL_DEPTH = 0.65` (line 170 `writeback_compile.py`). `li_star = round(0.65 * 36) = 23`. This is the **single construct/routing-write install layer**.
- `BAND = (0.60, 0.80)` (line 169). GD LoRA operates across layers `L22..L29` (confirmed in results.json: `"band": [22,23,24,25,26,27,28,29]`).
- So: **construct arm installs at L23 only; GD arm trains LoRA across L22–L29.**

### How the code appends FFN neurons / installs a plate

Both scripts use an identical surgery pattern (`append_neurons` / `restore_neurons`). The target is `dec[li_star].mlp` which is a SwiGLU block with three linear projections: `gate_proj` (d_model→d_ffn), `up_proj` (d_model→d_ffn), `down_proj` (d_ffn→d_model).

**`append_neurons(neurons)` at `writeback_compile.py` lines 654–676, `routing_register.py` lines 417–442:**

For each neuron `(k_unit, ref, down_col_vec)`:
- Append row `(4.0/ref) * k` to `gate_proj.weight` (sets gate pre-activation = 4.0 when input has projection `ref` onto `k`).
- Append row `(1.0/ref) * k` to `up_proj.weight` (sets up pre-activation = 1.0 at the same alignment).
- Append column `down_col_vec` to `down_proj.weight`.

The SwiGLU neuron output is `silu(gate_row · x) * (up_row · x) * down_col`. At `x_on = ref * k_unit`, gate pre-activation = 4.0, up pre-activation = 1.0, so output ≈ `silu(4.0) * 1.0 * down_col ≈ 0.982 * down_col` (near-unity firing). At `x_off ⊥ k`, both pre-activations ≈ 0, output ≈ 0. The 4/1 split ensures the gate drives the silu into saturation while the up-projection stays linear.

**`restore_neurons()`** slices back to `[:ff_orig]` on gate/up and `[:, :ff_orig]` on down, with `out_features`/`in_features` reset. No copy of original weights is needed since the originals are always the first `ff_orig` rows.

### Country key construction (build_keys / shared-Σ whitening)

Defined at `writeback_compile.py` lines 622–648, mirrored in `routing_register.py` lines 386–415.

Steps:
1. **Own activations**: for each country `c`, run `CC_FRAMES` templates (country-name-centric sentences, from `bake_stack.CC_FRAMES`) through `capture_postnorm()` → activations at L23 post-attention-layernorm, last token position.
2. **Innocent activations**: `PROSE_INNOCENTS` (non-geographic sentences) plus `DIRECT_PROMPT.format(lm=nc)` for 3 `NONCE_CANDS` (non-existent landmark prompts) — these set the null distribution for what a "no country" activation looks like.
3. **Shared covariance Σ**: computed over ALL own activations of all countries + all innocent activations pooled together. This is **shared-Σ whitening** — not per-class covariance.
4. **Regularization**: `cov += whiten_eps * (trace(cov)/d) * I` (default `whiten_eps=0.1`).
5. **Key**: `k_c = Σ^{-1} * (μ_own_c - μ_pop)`, then **unit-normalized**. This is the linear-discriminant direction in whitened space.
6. **ref**: `float(mean(own_activations @ k_c))` — the expected projection of a country-c activation onto its key.
7. **inn_max**: `float(max(innocent_activations @ k_c))` — worst-case innocent projection; separation = `ref - inn_max`.

The key `k_c` is a **whitened country discriminant direction in post-attention-layernorm activation space at L23**. The firing condition for the neuron is that `(post_attn_layernorm_output @ k_c) ≈ ref`.

### routing_write arm: address, value, strength, and WHY it went INERT

**Address (gate/up rows)**: same as construct — `(4.0/ref) * k_c` and `(1.0/ref) * k_c` — so the neuron fires (at saturation) when the post-attn-layernorm output at L23 projects `≈ ref` onto `k_c`.

**Value (down column)**: In routing_register, instead of `gain * unembed_dir(capital)`, it writes:

```python
S * unit(ternarize_vec(unembed_dir(capital)))
```

`ternarize_vec` (lines 75–82 of `routing_register.py`) is per-element TWN: `theta = 0.7 * mean(|v|)`, elements with `|v| > theta` are kept at their sign, others zeroed, resulting in a sparse `{-1, 0, +1}`-sign vector scaled by `gamma = mean(|v[mask]|)`. The result is then unit-normalized.

**Strength S**: `S = float(np.median(np.linalg.norm(dn_w, axis=0)))` where `dn_w = mlp.down_proj.weight[:, :ff_orig]` — the **median L2-norm of native down_proj columns at L23** (lines 453–455, `routing_register.py`). This anchors the new neuron's output to the natural scale of existing down_proj columns — it is a register-scale match, not a tuned gain.

**WHY routing_write went INERT** (from docstring lines 10–15 of `routing_register.py`, confirmed by gate output):

The s303 `construct` arm wrote `gain * unembed_dir(capital)` as the down_col, where `gain` was calibrated by a 2-iteration closed loop to achieve a **mean capital-logit boost of `DELTA_TARGET = 3.0`** on COUNTRY frames (not landmark frames). The calibration converged to `gain ≈ 0.3` (the throttle mentioned throughout), well below any threshold needed to move first-token argmax. The neuron **fired correctly** (key separation was demonstrated in s294), but the value write had magnitude ≈ 0.3 × ‖unembed_dir‖ ≈ 0.3, which in bfloat16 at L23 produced a logit delta far below the gap between the correct capital and the argmax city. Results confirm: `construct` is **byte-identical to base** on all splits (TRAIN=0.200, B1=0.125, B2=0.545 for both).

The deeper reason (per the routing_register docstring): construct wrote in the **MAGNITUDE register** (continuous graded value, calibrated gain). The fix is to write in the **ROUTING register** (ternary sign, full host-register strength S). The country key does fire; the problem is purely the write amplitude.

**routing_write also went INERT** (verdict `WRITE-INERT`, G1=False): the ternary write at strength S did not clear G1 on either B1 or B2. The advisory `boost_on_country_frames` value (line 516, routing_register.py) was expected to diagnose whether the write landed even on COUNTRY frames. Per the s304 pre-reg docstring (line 41–42): "attribute an INERT verdict: weak-write vs no-routing."

---

## C. THE HOOK MACHINERY

### What exists

**`capture_postnorm(prompts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray`** — defined at `writeback_compile.py` line 607, mirrored at `routing_register.py` line 377. This is the **only hook mechanism in both scripts**.

The implementation (lines 607–619 `writeback_compile.py`):

```python
def capture_postnorm(prompts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
    vecs = []
    for p in prompts:
        store = {}
        hnd = dec[li_star].post_attention_layernorm.register_forward_hook(
            lambda m, i, o, s=store: s.__setitem__("v", o))
        ids = tok(p, return_tensors="pt").to(dev)
        with torch.no_grad():
            model(**ids)
        hnd.remove()
        vecs.append(store["v"][0, -1, :].float().cpu().numpy())
    return np.stack(vecs)
```

- **Hook target**: `dec[li_star].post_attention_layernorm` at a **fixed, pre-selected layer** (`li_star = 23`).
- **What is captured**: the **output** of the post-attention layer norm (i.e., the MLP input at that layer), specifically **last token position** (`[0, -1, :]`), converted to float32 numpy.
- **Lifecycle**: hook is registered, one full forward pass runs (with `torch.no_grad()`), hook is removed. **One forward pass per prompt.** This is a **precompute** operation, not in-forward.
- **Usage**: called in `build_keys()` to collect own/innocent activations for whitening, and in `eval_cells()` (line 731, `writeback_compile.py`) to compute the detector readout.

### Critical answer for P-FAST-PLATE: what exists vs what is needed

**What exists**: `capture_postnorm` is a **precomputation hook** — it registers a hook, runs a complete forward pass, removes the hook, and returns the captured tensor as numpy. It **cannot** be used to write a delta-rule during a generation step because:
1. It is always called as a standalone forward pass with `torch.no_grad()`, not hooked into an autoregressive generation loop.
2. The hook captures once and is immediately removed.
3. The resulting numpy array is used only for key construction (a one-time precompute) or for the scalar detector score (also precomputed per prompt before scoring).

**What does NOT exist**: There is no mechanism in either script that:
- Intercepts a residual stream activation **mid-generation** (i.e., at decode step k while generating token k+1).
- Writes a fast-weight update (a delta to any weight matrix) **conditionally** on the value of an activation seen during that forward pass.
- Hooks multiple layers or carries state across decode steps.

**What P-FAST-PLATE needs** (in-forward delta-rule): At L23, during the forward pass for the one-shot landmark prompt, read the post-attn-layernorm output at the last token position (exactly as `capture_postnorm` does), compute `projection = activation @ k_c` for each country key, identify the active country (or apply a soft weighted combination), then **write** the corresponding capital value into the residual stream at that same step — all within one forward pass, before the logits are read. The mechanism to achieve this is a `register_forward_hook` on `dec[23].post_attention_layernorm` that:
1. Reads `output[0, -1, :]` (the MLP input).
2. Computes the fast-weight term: `Σ_c silu(4/ref_c * (act @ k_c)) * (1/ref_c * (act @ k_c)) * down_col_c`.
3. Adds it to the MLP output (i.e., hooks `dec[23].mlp` output instead, or uses a hook on the full layer's output).

The `capture_postnorm` pattern provides exactly the correct hook attachment point (`dec[li_star].post_attention_layernorm`) and the correct tensor index (`[0, -1, :]`). The gap is that `capture_postnorm` runs a **separate forward pass** to read the activation and returns numpy — it never writes back into the live computation graph. P-FAST-PLATE needs to hook the **same point** but **modify the ongoing forward pass** by injecting the fast-weight contribution into the MLP output (or residual). No infrastructure for that write-back currently exists in either file.

---

## D. THE GATES + SCORING

### `verbum.dsp.gate` and `paired_permutation`

Imported from `verbum.dsp` in both scripts. `gate(value, null_distribution, predict, alpha, name)` returns a `Gated` dataclass with fields: `name, value, null_name, null_mean, null_std, n_draws, predict, alpha, p, sign_ok, verdict, warnings`. `paired_permutation(a, b, rng)` generates the null distribution (10k draws) by randomly flipping signs of `(a - b)` per element and computing mean differences — this is the paired permutation test. Both `a` and `b` must be aligned per-cell arrays.

### Gate computation in `score_arms` / `score` (writeback_compile.py lines 241–291, routing_register.py lines 100–158)

The helper `_g(a, b, rng, alpha, name)` wraps `gate(mean(a-b), paired_permutation(a, b, rng), "greater", alpha, name=name)`. Gates fire iff `p < alpha` AND `mean(a-b) > 0` (sign_ok + verdict).

**G1 WIRE** (`alpha/3` each sub-gate, Bonferroni):
- `G1[B1]`: `_g(acc[arm]["B1"], acc["base"]["B1"], rng, alpha/3, ...)` → verdict AND flip (arm mean > base mean).
- `G1[B2]`: same for B2.
- `G1 = G1[B1].verdict AND G1[B1].flip AND G1[B2].verdict AND G1[B2].flip`.

**G2 NOT-LOOKUP** (`alpha/3`):
- `_g(acc[arm]["B2"], acc["construct_lookup"]["B2"], rng, alpha/3, ...)` — arm must exceed the materialized-view baseline on B2.

**G3 SPECIFICITY** (`alpha/3`):
- `_g(concatenate([acc[arm]["B1"], acc[arm]["B2"]]), concatenate([acc[shuffle]["B1"], acc[shuffle]["B2"]]), rng, alpha/3, ...)` — arm on held-out must exceed its own shuffle null.
- For `construct`: shuffle = `construct_shuffle` (deranged capitals, same keys/gain).
- For `gd_cd`: shuffle = `gd_shuffle` (deranged countries in teacher CoT).
- For `routing_write`: shuffle = `routing_shuffle` (deranged capitals, same keys/S/ternary).

**G5 SURVIVE** (no paired permutation — deterministic checks):
- `ce[arm] <= ce["base"] * 1.02` — innocent CE within 2% relative.
- `gh[arm][0] >= gh["base"][0] - 0.10` — g-hop accuracy within 0.10 absolute.
- `gh[arm][1] >= gh["base"][1] - 0.10` — h-hop accuracy within 0.10 absolute.

**G4 (PIN)** is advisory only — the `detector` field (whitened country readout) is collected per-cell in `eval_cells(keys_for_detector)` but is not gated; it appears in `detector_g4` in the results.

**Primaries G1–G3 use Bonferroni-corrected `alpha/3`; G5 uses deterministic thresholds.**

### Verdict assignment (`verdict_of`, `writeback_compile.py` lines 292–318, `routing_register.py` lines 143–152)

**writeback_compile.py:**
1. `VOID (gate-0)` if gate0_ok = False.
2. `VOID (lookup null moves B2 — task has a shortcut)` if `lookup_b2_moves` = True (the null itself clears G1 on B2 — task design flaw).
3. `HOST-DAMAGED` if no arm passes G5.
4. `WIRE-COMPILES (+BOTH)` if both construct and gd_cd pass G1∧G2∧G3∧G5.
5. `WIRE-COMPILES (+CONSTRUCTION-SUFFICES)` if only construct passes.
6. `WIRE-COMPILES (+GD-REQUIRED)` if only gd_cd passes.
7. `UNSPECIFIC` if some arm passes G1∧G2 but not G3.
8. `LOOKUP-ONLY` if arm passes train_up but not held_up (or passes held_up but not G2).
9. `STILL-EXTERNAL` if nothing moves even on TRAIN.

**routing_register.py** (simpler, single arm):
1. `VOID (gate-0)` / `HOST-DAMAGED` / `WRITE-SUFFICES` (G1∧G2∧G3∧G5) / `WRITE-DEGRADES` (G1 but ¬G3 or ¬G2) / `WRITE-INERT` (¬G1).

### Actual s303 verdict

`WIRE-COMPILES (+GD-REQUIRED)`. Construct: G1=False, G2=False, G3=False, G5=True (INERT). gd_cd: G1=True (B1: value=0.8125, p<0.0001; B2: value=0.455, p=0.0009), G2=True (value=0.409, p=0.0028), G3=True (value=0.807, p<0.0001), G5=True. lookup_b2_moves=False.

### `--validate` structure

`run_validate()` at `writeback_compile.py` lines 334–461, `routing_register.py` lines 162–265. Exercises:
1. Bank integrity (cell counts, shortcut-free, B2 isolation, first-word uniqueness).
2. Derangement correctness (no fixed points, bijection).
3. Whitened filter planted separation (synthetic vectors).
4. Neuron surgery equivalence on a tiny SwiGLU (checks `e_on < 1e-4` and `e_off < 1e-4`).
5. LoRA identity-at-init + grad isolation (writeback_compile only).
6. Planted verdict worlds — several `(TRAIN, B1, B2)` probability tuples generate synthetic `acc` arrays and verify that `score_arms` + `verdict_of` produce the expected verdict string. All use `np.random.default_rng` with fixed seeds; no model is loaded.

Returns 0 iff all checks pass, else 1.

### results.json schema

Top-level keys (from actual file): `model_id`, `config` (full argparse namespace), `install_layer` (int, 23), `band` (list of ints), `gate0` ({`ok`, `splits`, `cot_rate`}), `arms` (dict arm→{`seeds`: [[per-cell rows]], `ce`, `gh`, optionally `gain`/`boost`}), `scoring` ({`gates`, `verdict`, `detector_g4`}).

Per-cell row in `seeds[i]`: `{landmark, country, split, truth, arg, correct, margin, detector}`.

### Is writeback_compile.py import-safe?

**Yes, fully guarded.** Lines 975–976:

```python
if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

The `run_validate` and `run_model` functions are inside `def` bodies; `LoRALinear` is defined inside a `try/except` block (lines 465–490) that safely degrades to `None` if torch is absent. All model-loading and torch imports are inside `run_model()`. **Importing `writeback_compile` as a module executes only** the module-level constants (`BANK`, `TRAIN_COUNTRIES`, `B2_COUNTRIES`, `SPLITS`, `DIRECT_PROMPT`, `TEACHER_PROMPT`, etc.) and the `from bake_stack / fn_stack / holo_cap / holo_frag / native_compose_check` imports plus `from verbum.dsp import gate, paired_permutation`. No model is loaded, no torch is required at import time.

`routing_register.py` is similarly guarded (line 634: `if __name__ == "__main__": raise SystemExit(main())`). Its `run_model` has all torch imports deferred inside the function body.

### Summary for pre-registration

The single most important mechanistic fact: **`capture_postnorm` is a precomputation hook** — it runs a separate, complete `torch.no_grad()` forward pass to read `dec[li_star].post_attention_layernorm` output at the last token position, then removes the hook. It never writes into a live forward pass. **P-FAST-PLATE's in-forward delta rule requires a hook on `dec[23].mlp`'s output (or equivalently, on the full decoder layer output) that reads the MLP input at the same step, computes the fast-weight contribution dynamically using the materialized country activation, and adds it to the residual — all within a single forward pass.** No such mechanism exists in either script. The reuse surface (key construction, neuron geometry, gate scoring, cell/split machinery, verdict logic) is entirely available via `import writeback_compile as wb`.

## 4. Recursion completes it — the standalone machine

Recursing ordinary weights = iterating one fixed f. Recursing a **plate** =
each pass, the state illuminates the weights; only the Bragg-matched grating
diffracts; the state selects the program, the program transforms the state.
**The recursion is a fetch–decode–execute cycle:**

```
plate (ternary, superposed)      = program memory   [crystal=ISA; FN-INDEX dispatch ✓]
residual state                   = accumulator      [the light field]
mirrors, per-pass schedule       = ALU sign ops; pass index = program counter
recursion of same weights        = Y, finally native
sign() projection between passes = INTERNAL COLLAPSE (tape without tokens)
Δx < ε (WHNF)                    = halt — halting IS the semantics
```

**Three consequences:**

1. **The recursion family gets a home.** Flat models fake Y with unrolled
   depth → recursion family never binds above null (no loop to bind to).
   A real loop should give it a home → P-LOOP-BINDS (below).
2. **Ternarization between passes = an internal tape.** sign() is a
   discretizer; each pass writes a crisp state and re-illuminates from it =
   CoT internalized into the weight register (reduce → collapse →
   regenerate inside the forward loop). Rung-3b's target ("teach an internal
   collapse") becomes an architectural primitive instead of a training goal.
3. **Holography makes tiny sufficient.** Superposition ⇒ capacity is
   program-count not program-size (CAP: coherent-gain, not crosstalk decay);
   bounded weights + unbounded compute (depth from recursion) = λ smallest
   realized mechanically.

**Halt-semantics differentiator vs literature:** Universal Transformer / ACT
/ PonderNet halt on a learned confidence scalar. Ours halts on **Δx < ε ≡
normal form reached** — and handles Ω correctly for free (a true reducer
keeps Δx high on non-normalizing input; non-termination under a K-budget is
correct behavior). Deadband target Δx* ≈ 0.24 (fp-decay probe) is what makes
it trainable without collapsing the bought depth.

**Deployment modes:** transducer mode (level 3 — attached; host supplies
light/collapse/loop) · standalone mode (level 4 — own loop; recursed plates
+ mirrors + sign-collapse + Δx-halt). Same rung as the pythia-14m
seeded-scratch pair: the construct path and the recursion close into one
architecture — **the level-4 door**.

### Thinking

The key issue is that the routing mechanism becomes inactive because the country intermediate isn't materialized when using the one-shot landmark prompt—the static plate creates a country-to-capital connection at layer 23 that activates based on the country key projection, but this breaks down on the direct prompt variant where the setup differs.

The puzzle deepens when I consider that gate-0 still requires the host to compute the country somewhere, yet it's not appearing in the specific layer/token/representation space where the static key addresses it. The country exists in the computation graph in some distributed form, but not materialized at the exact location the plate expects. This is where P-FAST-PLATE's in-forward writing should theoretically help—by having access to whatever intermediate representation the forward pass actually produces—though I need to think through exactly how that resolves the materialization problem.

There are two ways to interpret P-FAST-PLATE: either it's just a hook-based reimplementation of the same static routing logic (which would be equally inert if the country key never fires), or it's something more fundamental—writing the fast-weight at a different layer or time where the country IS actually materialized, then reading it later. The delta-rule fast-weight programmer idea suggests the latter: during autoregressive generation, the model must compute the country at some point to eventually produce the capital, so the mechanism would be to capture that intermediate during the forward pass and use it to write the fast-weight at the right moment.

But in a one-shot scoring scenario with greedy first-token argmax, there's only a single forward pass producing one next-token distribution—no "later token" to read the fast-weight at. So the write and read would have to happen within the same forward pass, meaning the fast-weight gets written at an early layer where the country representation exists and then read at a deeper layer.

The core tension is that the country is latent—it exists somewhere in the forward pass since the model can solve the task—but the question becomes whether there's a specific layer where the country becomes materialized enough that a fast-weight mechanism could capture and propagate it to the output. The static key was built from country-NAME frames, but when the model infers the country from a landmark, the internal representation lives in a different subspace than the named representation. So the static key addresses the wrong geometry. A forward-etched plate could theoretically read the country in its own materialized geometry, but that requires a key derived from the landmark prompts themselves to match what's actually being produced.

The real insight is that a delta-rule fast-weight mechanism could write an association during the forward pass using the LIVE, materialized country representation as the key — not a pre-baked named-country key. This way the write captures whatever geometry the model has actually produced while processing the landmark prompt, and the same forward pass can then read back the capital value using that same live representation.

But there's a circularity problem: to write the association you need to know which capital goes with the materialized country, which requires either a pre-computed country→capital table or a way to match the live country direction against reference country directions. So the mechanism would need to read the materialized country from the residual, match it against a set of reference country directions (either live or precomputed), and then write the delta-weight using the best-matching capital and the live country direction as the key.

The real question is whether this matching would succeed where the static named-country key failed. If the landmark prompt inference can build reference country directions from the landmark geometry itself rather than from named-country frames, then matching against those references might work even when the static approach didn't. So the honest experiment would be to build country keys directly from the landmark prompts and measure whether the country materializes at a deeper layer or later in the residual stream than expected. the one that actually matters. The static-landmark-key approach is cheaper to test and would tell me whether routing_write was failing because of bad key geometry, but the true fast-weight mechanism is where the write happens dynamically during the forward pass using whatever activations the current test cell produces — that's the untested piece I need to probe. comes materialized as a concrete representation, the fast-plate can inject the capital directly into the forward pass, bypassing the need for the h-hop to fire naturally. This way, the model doesn't have to emit the country token explicitly—the fast-plate bridges the gap by recognizing when the country representation exists internally and routing to the correct capital before the h-hop machinery would normally activate. The fast-plate doesn't need to be a learnable weight at all—it's really just an activation-steering operation that reads the noisy country from the residual stream at one layer, performs a cleanup (snapping it to the nearest stored direction), and writes the cleaned country back upstream so the host's native circuitry can do the rest. This generalizes to B2 automatically since the host already knows how to map countries to capitals; the plate just ensures the country signal is crisp enough to trigger that mapping reliably.

The key empirical question is whether the country is even decodable on the landmark prompt at some layer before we intervene. The gate measurement uses a different prompt ("landmark is located in" → country), but on the direct prompt itself, can we extract the country from any layer's activations? That's what I need to measure first—it's the crux of whether this approach will work at all.

So the fast-plate experiment has a clear structure: first, a characterization phase where I scan the training landmarks on direct prompts across all layers to find where country information is linearly decodable, if anywhere. That's a read-only measurement with no gradients, and I report the best layer and token position. Then at that identified layer, I apply the cleanup-and-reinject mechanism.

I need to be careful about the λ threshold and avoid tuning just to pass the test. The characterization phase must stay frozen on the training set before touching the test splits, and I should include both a shuffled null baseline (scrambled country cleanup) and a static named-country control to show that any win comes from the live geometry rather than just having country information available.

But I'm getting ahead of myself with the design details. This is a significant experimental proposal, and I should present the full design to Michael for approval before freezing anything—the process requires human sign-off on the approach, not just my unilateral decision.

I'm also realizing there's real mechanical ambiguity about what P-FAST-PLATE actually does. There are at least three candidate approaches I'm considering: a cleanup-reinject mechanism that reads the latent country and snaps it back upstream, a delta-rule fast-weight relay that writes weight updates from live activations, or something else entirely.

The cleanup-reinject approach feels like the strongest design, with the others serving as contrast conditions. But before committing to any of these, I need to run a mandatory read-only pre-check: is the country even materialized anywhere on the one-shot prompt? If it's not, the whole mechanism fails and we've learned something decisive about the exhaustion law. That pre-characterization is so cheap and informative that it should probably be the first thing I test.

Now I'm drafting this as a design proposal for Michael—laying out the mechanization choices and asking for his preference before we freeze anything.

## What P-FAST-PLATE must actually be — the sharp reframing grounding forced

The triangulation says construction is INERT in **both** registers because **the country intermediate is unmaterialized on the one-shot LANDMARK prompt** — `activation @ k_c ≈ 0` at L23, so any *static* neuron (magnitude or routing) never fires. The routing_write key was even built from country-**NAME** frames ("France is a country…"), a different geometry than the country-when-inferred-from-a-landmark.

The machine page §5c says the forward-etched plate is "the only mechanism with access to the materialized intermediate." Grounding sharpens *what that means here*: there is only **one** forward pass and we score the **first** token (no later token to diffract off). So "in-forward" can only buy us a **within-pass read-then-reinject across layers**. And critically — this lands **exactly** on the s300 resolution you and I already reached: *"internalize the PIN — give the walker an internal cleanup memory."*

## Recommended design: P-FAST-PLATE as an in-forward **cleanup-and-reinject** (not a capital-writer)

The honest, highest-signal mechanization — and the one genuinely different from the two INERT arms:

1. **Read** the residual at the layer where the country is *most materialized on the landmark prompt* (measured, TRAIN-only, read-only — see the gating step below), last token.
2. **Cleanup**: snap that latent/noisy country to the nearest stored country direction (Hopfield/HRR cleanup over the 8 TRAIN country directions — a sign-vote/argmax collapse; this IS the "internal collapse" organ).
3. **Reinject** the *crisp* country direction back into the residual, **upstream** of the host's native country→capital (h-hop) circuit.
4. The **host's own h-hop** produces the capital — which gate-0 already proves works when the country is present (`h_ok`).

Why this is the right shape:
- **No capital table** → it generalizes to **B2 (held-country) for free**, because the host knows all capitals; the plate only stores/cleans *country* directions. That makes B2 a clean wire-vs-lookup test as before.
- It **creates** a materialized intermediate (the crisp country) that the pass didn't hold — the exact thing static plates provably can't do.
- It operationalizes s300's "pin = internal cleanup between traversal edges" and §5c's fast-plate as a **write done by the pass**.

## The decisive, cheap, read-only pre-gate (I want this in the pre-reg)

Before any cleanup: **is the country linearly decodable anywhere on the one-shot DIRECT_PROMPT?** Sweep layers/token (TRAIN only), frozen. Two branches:
- **Materialized somewhere** → cleanup is live; run the arms at that layer.
- **Never materialized** (truly latent, s295 airtight) → P-FAST-PLATE is dead *a priori by measurement* → verdict **STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT**, a real finding (the exhaustion law is mechanistic, not incidental). We'd stop before burning the full run.

Arms: `base` · `fast_plate` (cleanup-reinject) · `fast_plate_shuffle` (deranged country cleanup = λ yardstick null) · `static_landmark_key` (a static neuron with keys sourced from TRAIN *landmark* prompts instead of name-frames — the contrast that tells us whether routing_write failed on *geometry* vs on *materialization*). Gates F1 wire / F2 not-lookup / F3 specificity-vs-shuffle / F5 survive, reusing the frozen 53 gate-0 cells + verbum.dsp machinery.

### Thinking

Michael went with cleanup-reinject, so now I'm drafting the full §P-FAST-PLATE pre-registration document. The workflow is straightforward—I write it up, he reviews and approves the freeze, then I commit it.

Let me work through the core design: I'm setting up a materialization scan gate that runs on training landmarks to measure whether a linear probe can decode the country from residual activations at each layer, which tells us if the country information is accessible at that point in the network.

The key constraint is finding the right layer L* — it needs to be early enough that country is decodable but before the capital gets locked in downstream, so we have room to reinject the operand. I'm also building in a decodability threshold that compares against a shuffled baseline to ensure the signal is real and not just noise.

There's an important distinction though: the gate test on the country-eliciting prompt proves the host can retrieve the country when prompted directly, but that's different from whether it's actually materialized as an intermediate representation on the direct one-shot prompt. The materialization scan will tell us whether the country is genuinely present in the residual stream at some layer, which is what we need to know for the surgery to work.

For the tap point itself, I'm thinking the residual stream output from a decoder layer is the cleanest place to read from — it's more direct than pulling from the post-attention layer norm. ers where the host's h-hop fires. For the cleanup mechanism, I'm storing country directions from the eight training countries, computed from their landmark activations at layer L* using whitening and unit normalization. At test time, I read the activation, project it onto each stored direction, then collapse to a single country using a hard argmax (treating it like a sign-vote, which aligns with the pin/WHNF framing) with a confidence threshold. The reinjected vector needs to encode the crisp country direction in a form that propagates through the remaining layers.

The key question is whether reinjecting the country's own materialized direction—its prototype at layer L* scaled by activation strength—will cause the host to produce the correct capital. This hinges on whether L* sits upstream of the h-hop and whether the prototype direction is what the h-hop actually reads. I could also try reinjecting using the country-name geometry instead, since the h-hop was likely trained to extract country information from name tokens.

Actually, I think the cleaner approach is to read the country in its landmark-materialized geometry (where it gets recognized via the landmark features) but then write it back in the named geometry (where the host's h-hop expects to find it). That way the circuit can properly fire—the static plate couldn't do this because it only tried to read in one geometry.

The recognition keys come from the landmark activations during training, and the reinjection values are the country-name-frame prototype scaled to strength S. But here's the problem: for held-out B2 countries, there's no stored recognition key from training, so the cleanup mechanism can't actually identify which country it's looking at—that breaks generalization entirely.

Wait, though—the routing_write arm built keys for all 16 countries from the name-frames, including B2 countries, since the country names themselves are known. The held-out-ness is about the landmarks and composition, not the country names. So the real question is whether the model can internalize the compositional rule: landmark fires → country key activates → capital gets written, and whether that country-to-capital mapping generalizes to the held-out countries.

For cleanup-reinject, I'm facing a symmetry problem: I could build recognition keys for all 16 countries, but the source matters—if I build them from country-name frames, that's the same geometry that already failed.  If I use landmark activations, I only have TRAIN countries to sample from, leaving B2 without data. The key insight is that if the landmark-inferred country representation matched the name-frame representation, then name-frame keys would have worked and routing_write wouldn't have been inert—but it was, so the geometries genuinely differ. This means B2 recognition is fundamentally hard for cleanup too. I'm considering whether to accept that cleanup cleanly generalizes only to B1 (held landmarks within TRAIN countries) and treat B2 as a stretch goal, reframing the wire test around B1 as the primary benchmark.

But that approach weakens the distinction between wire and lookup—B1 alone can't cleanly separate them since it could just be a TRAIN-country lookup. A better path is to learn a linear map from name-frame geometry to landmark geometry using the TRAIN countries where I have both representations, then apply that transformation to the B2 name prototypes to construct landmark-space keys for all 16 countries.

This stays sharp for B2 and avoids backprop since Procrustes is closed-form, though it does introduce a learned linear map—defensible as measured geometry, like whitening. The downside is that Procrustes on just 8 points is thin, and the whole approach is getting complicated.

Alternatively, I could skip stored keys entirely and instead read the country directly using the host's own g-hop computation at materialization time, then project and snap to the nearest decodable country subspace.

The cleanest approach feels like building recognition keys from name-frames for all 16 countries and applying them at layer L* where the country actually materializes from landmarks. The hypothesis is that routing_write failed because it was reading at L23 with name-keys before landmark-materialization happened—so at the right layer, the landmark-inferred country should align well enough with the name-frame key for argmax recognition, even if not for a static always-on neuron. The failure was timing, not geometry.

But wait—if name-frame keys align with landmark-materialized country at L*, then a static name-key neuron at L* would also work, which means "in-forward" wouldn't be doing anything special beyond just routing_write at the correct layer. So I need to figure out what a dynamic hook actually adds over a static appended neuron. A static neuron fires whenever the projection aligns with the reference, always on and additive. A dynamic hook can do nonlinear operations like argmax across dimensions.

The static routing_write neuron uses the name-key as both read and write trigger, outputting the ternary capital—but it can't collapse multiple countries via winner-take-all since each neuron fires independently. The dynamic cleanup hook, though, can do winner-take-all collapse, decouple the read geometry from the write geometry, and reinject the country itself rather than just the capital. The most critical genuinely-dynamic ingredient is the winner-take-all nonlinearity.

The key difference is that even if recognition uses name-keys and the country materializes at the right layer, the static routing_write would write the capital directly without h-hop and fail, whereas the dynamic plate writes the country and leverages the host h-hop with a hard collapse—those are testable differences. I'm wondering whether routing_write at the correct layer with capital-writing would be the cleaner test of whether it was just a layer and register mismatch, though routing_write already swept through those variations.

Let me step back and focus on the essential, falsifiable experiment without over-engineering. The cleanest approach is to capture post-attention-layernorm activations at every layer during training on landmark prompts, build per-country prototypes from the training countries, and measure how decodable each country is per layer against a null baseline.

The key metrics are: finding the layer where country decodability peaks (L*_dec), checking whether the landmark geometry aligns with the name-frame geometry at that layer using cosine similarity, and determining if any layer beats the deranged-label null. If no layer exceeds the null threshold, that's a clean finding that the country is never materialized from the one-shot prompt. Otherwise, I pick the decodable layer deterministically—choosing the highest decodability in the lower two-thirds of the stack to leave room for higher-level processing.

At the materialized layer L*, I'm hooking into the residual output at the last token, whitening the activation using the shared covariance, then recognizing the country by taking the argmax projection against the name-frame recognition keys (applied across all 16 countries). I'm gating on confidence—only firing if the top projection clears an innocent-null floor derived from the prose innocents and nonce, so that innocents don't trigger false cleanup.

Then I'm reinjecting by adding a scaled unit vector of the recognized country's named-geometry prototype back into the residual, where the scale is the median native residual norm matched to the routing write. This lets the host continue and produce the capital via its native h-hop pathway.

I'm testing several arms: the base approach, the fast_plate variant described above, a fast_plate_shuffle that deliberately scrambles the recognition-to-reinject mapping to destroy routing and should fail, and a static_reinject control. Now I'm setting up the gate conditions across the five primary factors, using Bonferroni correction to control for multiple comparisons. F1 checks if the wiring mechanism beats baseline on both conditions, F2 validates that the lookup isn't what's driving performance, and F3 tests specificity on held-out data to confirm the effect generalizes. F5 ensures the model stays honest with low error rates and consistent accuracy across groups, while F4 remains an advisory metric comparing the collapse mechanisms rather than a hard gate.

I'm also examining decodability at the critical layer, checking landmark-versus-name similarity, and looking at recognition accuracy on the training set to understand what's actually being learned. The verdicts are now frozen: measurement itself appears to be the bottleneck preventing the country variable from materializing, the fast-plate wiring combined with collapse load-bearing is what installs the cleanup in the forward pass, and the nonlinear collapse mechanism is the load-bearing component that makes it work. Without F3, the routing bypasses the map entirely. F5 is also absent, which means the host layer is compromised. Looking at the routing_write docstring more carefully: if the country key never fires on the one-shot LANDMARK prompt because it's never materialized at any layer, then M fails and we're stuck external (~45%). But if the country is materialized somewhere but the static plate reads from the wrong layer or targets the wrong field, that's a wiring issue—

either COLLAPSE-LOAD-BEARING or GEOMETRY-SUFFICES (~35%). If materialization happens but reinjection can't drive the one-shot host hop, that's FAST-PLATE-INERT (~20%). My honest read leans toward STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT because gate-0's g_ok used a country-eliciting prompt, which is much easier than having the direct prompt materialize the country on its own; the s295 exhaustion law suggests the one-shot prompt doesn't hold that intermediate state. If M actually passes, that would be the real surprise. The materialization scan is the key test here.

Now I need to present this frozen design to Michael concisely for the freeze go-ahead. I should also verify the s222 recipe elements are locked in: register-matched strength S and at least 3 derangement seeds for the shuffle null (since the result is deterministic given the frozen keys, seeds only matter for resampling innocents or the shuffle variations). Gate-0 needs to be checked too.

For the reinject layer, I'm keeping it simple: single layer at L*, one hook reading post-attention layer norm and writing to the residual. The key insight is that using recognition keys from name-frames at L* is exactly what could make static_reinject work too, which is fine—that's precisely what the FAST-PLATE-WIRES versus COLLAPSE-LOAD-BEARING fork is designed to measure.

### §P-FAST-PLATE — pre-reg (draft, awaiting freeze GO)

**Question.** Can an in-forward **cleanup-and-reinject** plate (no gradient) install the operand→capital linker by *materializing* the country intermediate the one-shot pass leaves latent — the one construction door static plates provably can't reach (s304 WRITE-INERT triangulation)?

**The mechanistic edge over routing_write (why it's genuinely different).** routing_write read at L23 in **named-country geometry** (where the landmark prompt doesn't materialize the country) and wrote the **capital** directly. P-FAST-PLATE inverts both: **read where the country is materialized-from-landmark, write the country in the geometry the host's own h-hop reads, and let the host make the capital.** Two static-plate-impossible operations: (1) nonlinear winner-take-all **collapse** (the s300 "pin" / §4 internal-collapse organ), (2) read-geometry ≠ write-geometry.

**MATERIALIZATION SCAN (read-only pre-gate M, TRAIN-only, frozen).** On TRAIN landmark DIRECT_PROMPTs, capture last-token post-attn-LN at every layer; measure leave-one-landmark-out country-decodability per layer vs a **deranged-label null** (gate). 
- **M fails** (no layer beats null) → the country is *never* materialized one-shot → **STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT** (s295 exhaustion law is mechanistic; stop — real finding). 
- **M passes** → `L*` = frozen rule: highest-decodability layer in the lower ⅔ of the stack (leaves h-hop room downstream).

**THE PLATE (single hook at L*, last token).** Whiten `a`; recognize `c* = argmax_c (â·k_c)` over all 16 name-frame keys with an **innocent-null confidence floor** (no fire on PROSE_INNOCENTS/nonce → protects F5); reinject `S·unit(v_{c*})` where `v_{c*}` = country **named**-geometry prototype at L*, `S` = median native down_proj col-norm (register-matched, as routing_write). Host continues → capital via native h-hop → **B2 generalizes free** (plate stores only *country*, host knows all capitals).

**Arms:** `base` · `fast_plate` · `fast_plate_shuffle` (recognize c*, reinject v_{derange(c*)}; matched strength; **λ yardstick**, ≥3 derangement seeds) · `static_reinject` (softmax always-on, **no** hard collapse — isolates whether the collapse is load-bearing) · `construct_lookup` (inherited F2 null, must fail B2).

**Gates** (verbum.dsp, Bonferroni α/3 on F1–F3):
- **F1 WIRE** — fast_plate > base, flip on B1 **and** B2
- **F2 NOT-LOOKUP** — fast_plate > construct_lookup on B2
- **F3 SPECIFICITY** — fast_plate > fast_plate_shuffle on held-out (load-bearing)
- **F5 SURVIVE** — innocent CE ≤2% rel base; g/h within 0.10
- *Advisory:* fast_plate vs static_reinject (collapse load-bearing?); decodability(L*); landmark-vs-name cosine; TRAIN recognition acc

**Verdicts:** STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT (¬M) · FAST-PLATE-WIRES **(+COLLAPSE-LOAD-BEARING)** (F1∧F2∧F3∧F5 ∧ > static_reinject) · FAST-PLATE-WIRES **(+GEOMETRY-SUFFICES)** (gates pass, ≈ static_reinject → it was a layer+target error, not a wall) · **FAST-PLATE-INERT** (M passes but ¬F1 → construction insufficient even in-forward → gradient uniquely required, the last door closed) · UNSPECIFIC (¬F3) · HOST-DAMAGED (¬F5).

**A-priori lean (grounded; do NOT peek).** I lean slightly toward **STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT** (~45%): gate-0's `g_ok` used a country-*eliciting* prompt (G_QUERY), far easier than the DIRECT prompt materializing the country unbidden — the whole s295 law predicts the one-shot prompt holds no episodic intermediate. If M *passes*, that is itself the surprise. Then FAST-PLATE-WIRES ~35% (split collapse-load-bearing vs geometry-suffices), FAST-PLATE-INERT ~20%. **Either M-branch is a real finding** — the scan makes the experiment informative even in failure.

**Frozen recipe (s222).** Reuse `import writeback_compile as wb` + `routing_register` helpers (no fork); frozen 53 gate-0 cells; L* from the frozen scan rule; S register-matched; ≥3 derangement seeds; Qwen3-4B, MPS, bf16. `--validate` planted worlds → smoke (mechanics only, s297) → GO → full run → frozen scoring.

routing_write read at L23 in **named-country geometry** (where the one-shot
landmark prompt does not materialize the country) and wrote the **capital**
directly → INERT. P-FAST-PLATE inverts both moves: **read where the country is
materialized-from-landmark, write the country in the geometry the host's own
h-hop reads, and let the host produce the capital.** Two operations a static
appended neuron cannot do: (1) nonlinear winner-take-all **collapse** (the s300
"pin between traversal edges" / §4 internal-collapse organ); (2) read-geometry ≠
write-geometry (decouple recognition from reinjection). Because the plate stores
only *country* (not capital), **B2 (held-country) generalizes free** — the host
knows all capitals via its native h-hop.

**MATERIALIZATION SCAN — read-only pre-gate M (TRAIN-only, FROZEN, hard stop).**
On TRAIN landmark DIRECT_PROMPTs, capture last-token post-attention-layernorm at
every layer; measure leave-one-landmark-out country-decodability per layer against
a **deranged-label null** (verbum.dsp gate).
- **M fails** (no layer's decodability beats the null at α) → the country is
  *never* linearly materialized on the one-shot prompt → **STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-
  MEASUREMENT**: the s295 exhaustion law is *mechanical*, not incidental; the
  in-forward door is closed by measurement. STOP (do not run the plate arms). A
  real finding either way — the scan makes the experiment informative even in
  failure.
- **M passes** → `L*` = frozen selection rule: the highest-decodability layer in
  the **lower ⅔ of the stack** (leaves h-hop room downstream). Ties → lowest layer.

**THE PLATE (single forward hook at L*, last token position).** For the live
activation `a`:
1. **Whiten** `a` with the shared-Σ from the scan → `â`.
2. **Recognize** `c* = argmax_c (â · k_c)` over all 16 country keys `{k_c}` built
   from country-NAME frames (CC_FRAMES) at L* — with an **innocent-null confidence
   floor**: fire only if the top projection exceeds the max innocent projection
   (PROSE_INNOCENTS + NONCE_CANDS at L*). No fire on innocents → protects F5.
3. **Reinject** `S · unit(v_{c*})` into the residual at L*, where `v_{c*}` = the
   country **named**-geometry prototype at L* and `S` = median native down_proj
   column-norm at L* (register-matched, as routing_write — no calibration loop).
4. Host continues → capital via native h-hop.

**Arms** (re-scored on the frozen 53 gate-0 cells; per-seed shuffle):
- `base` — floor (must reproduce 0.200 / 0.125 / 0.545).
- `fast_plate` — the cleanup-reinject above (hard argmax collapse + confidence floor).
- `fast_plate_shuffle` — **λ yardstick**: recognize `c*`, reinject `v_{derange(c*)}`

- `static_reinject` — **collapse-isolation**: a soft, always-on write

  NO confidence gate). If `fast_plate > static_reinject`, the nonlinear collapse is
