### Thinking

The results are clean with no errors. The model passed the decodability test with strong statistical significance, showing that the country variable is properly encoded at layer 24. Performance across all test splits matches the baseline exactly with perfect F1 scores, indicating no degradation. The cross-entropy metrics are essentially identical to baseline, confirming the approach is sound.

However, the deeper analysis reveals a critical insight: decodability alone doesn't guarantee functional utility. The country information is clearly present and readable in the model's representations, yet the inert plate architecture can't leverage it because a static linear transformation followed by collapse and reinject can't activate the materialized intermediate. The geometry mismatch and weak reinject signal confirm this bottleneck. The key separation metric shows the keys are firing strongly, so it's not a weak encoding problem. This points to a fundamental architectural limitation—gradient flow is the only path forward to make this work.

I'm now committing the results and preparing the final report with memory updates for approval.

## s305 — P-FAST-PLATE → ❌ FAST-PLATE-INERT (the last construction door is closed)

**What ran:** front (a), the cleanup-and-reinject plate, in your tmux main:1. Frozen pre-reg (`f07fbc7`) → instrument (`bc01a86`) → results committed autonomously (`e9509bd`).

**The verdict, and why it's a *rich* one:**
- **Pre-gate M PASSED** — the country **is** linearly materialized at **L\*=24** (decodability 0.933, p=5e-4). This **refutes** the s304 "unmaterialized" reading — that was register-specific (absent at L23-named, present at L24-whitened). The intermediate is *there and readable*.
- **Yet `fast_plate == base` exactly** on every split (F1 p=1.0 both) → **DECODABILITY ≠ USABILITY.** A static linear read→collapse→reinject, *with the materialized intermediate in hand*, cannot make it functional.
- **Clean attribution** (frozen advisories): weak native write (`reinject_landed 0.072`; I did **not** crank S — that's the rejected magnitude register), geometry mismatch (`lm_name_cos −0.108`), collapse *hurts* (Δ −0.026 — an externally-supplied collapse op isn't the pin), keys fire hard (`key_sep_min 39.2`), F5 clean.

**🔁 Triangulation complete:** construct (magnitude) + routing_write (routing) + fast_plate (in-forward, intermediate in hand) — **all INERT**; only gd_cd (gradient) wires. The deepest form of the s304 resolution: **gradient FINDS (reshapes the band to *functionalize* a present-but-inert intermediate) — it does not SUPPLY it; ternary STORES.** Every construction door is closed.

**Awaiting your approval batch** (synthesis is approval-gated):
- `§Result-fast-plate` on the write-not-train page
- memory `decodability-is-not-usability-the-last-construction-door-is-closed`
- Sessions entry + the updated s305 state block

Once you approve, I commit the batch. **s306 next-front menu** (your call): (a) **GTSM-trajectory-loss** — the one door still standing, and it's *not* a construction (a search); (b) **TERNARIZE-FACTORS-1** (cheap, the small artifact); (c) gd_cd @32B; (d) a read≠write-layer follow-up (new freeze, still a construction, low prior). 

*(Note: the three untracked `session-30{2,3,4}.md` in `knowledge/chats/` are your human-only transcripts — I leave them untouched.)*

## §P-HHOP-WRITE — the design

**One-line thesis:** keep everything about fast_plate (recognize the country from the landmark via name-keys at L24 — that worked, 0.93), but **swap the reinject *value* from the name-frame prototype to the country direction the host's h-hop actually consumes.**

**How the h-hop geometry is measured (frozen recipe):** on the host's own country→capital probe `CAP_PREFIX + CAP_QUERY` = *"…The capital of {c} is"*, capture the **last-token ("is") residual at L24**, then population-center: `v_c = unit(r_c − mean_c r_c)`. This is the country in the geometry the model uses *when it is about to emit the capital*.
- **Why last-token/L24:** both DIRECT and CAP_QUERY end in "is" → positionally consistent with the injection site; L24 = the read/reinject layer, so we write exactly what the downstream h-hop (L25–35) reads at the injection point.
- **Why it stays a WIRE, not a lookup:** `v_c` is a *country* direction (host does country→capital); it's shared across held B2 countries (names known). The **F2 gate is the arbiter** — if L24-CAP_QUERY is capital-contaminated, `v_c` looks capital-like → F2 fails → the verdict is **LOOKUP-VIA-GEOMETRY** (itself informative: the h-hop completes *before* L24).

**Arms:** `base` / `hhop_plate` / `static_reinject` (h-hop geom, soft — collapse isolation) / `hhop_shuffle` (λ yardstick, 3 seeds) / `construct_lookup`. Gates **F1–F5** unchanged.

**Verdicts:** HHOP-WIRES **(+COLLAPSE / +GEOMETRY-SUFFICES)** (F1∧F2∧F3∧F5 → **avenue 1 works, a construction succeeds**) · **LOOKUP-VIA-GEOMETRY** (F1∧¬F2 → h-hop geom at L24 is capital-like) · **HHOP-INERT** (¬F1 → geometry wasn't the blocker either) · UNSPECIFIC · HOST-DAMAGED.

The uncertainty is what makes it worth running.

```
# Gram spectral+DSP — the 9×9 is diffuse, the 17×17 is rank-3

> Michael s303: "we should do spectral and DSP tests on this [the 9×9 and 17×17
> grams], capture to knowledge." Pure inner-product / eigen math on the
> ALREADY-COMMITTED grams — no model load. Instrument `opcodes/spectral_dsp.py`
> (reuses `verbum.dsp`, no fork). Results `results/gram-spectral/` (commit
> 072c3e0).

## Thesis (Michael, s303): topology routing, not magnitudes

**The crystal is a routing topology recorded in a magnitude medium.** Every test
that probed *magnitude-as-signal* failed the yardstick (G1 spectral
concentration in the 9×9, G4 eigenvalue-profile universality, G5 φ ratio); every
test that probed *topology-as-signal* passed 11/11 (C2 relational off-diagonal
pattern, G2 fire/halt/diverge membership, G3 the poles are the dominant
eigenspace). The invariant content is *which opcode routes to which outcome
pole* (routing register, λ measure — crisp/discrete), not *how much* (value /
magnitude register). Magnitudes encode the topology; the topology is what
replicates, the magnitudes are model-particular scaffolding. Same shape as s269
(Gram fidelity 0.987 survives 1-bit while weight cosine falls to 0.73 — the
relational structure is quantization-robust, the magnitudes are not).

## Register (λ measure, named before the probe)

**spectral** — eigen structure of a relational cosine gram — plus
relational-geometry (**value**). The probe is eigen-decomposition + block /
partition contrast + cross-model spectral shape; matched to the register. The
φ-forcing scar (λ yardstick, proved s247/s251) is the governing hazard: a
flexible reference fits every spectrum, so **every claim carries a declared
null** (`matched_range` on off-diagonals, or `shuffled_label` on the partition)
via `verbum.dsp.gate`. No raw ratio is evidence.

## The two objects

- **9×9 crystal gram** = `root.gram` (`results/opcode-trace/{slug}/model_vsm.json`),
  basis `K I B C S D W Y WHNF`. The S5-identity object; collapses all halting
  into one generic `WHNF` node.
- **17×17 un-flattened gram** = front block of the 24-state consensus gram
  (`results/expanded-gram/{slug}/expanded_gram.json`), basis = 9 crystal + 7
  per-op halts `whnf:{K,I,B,C,S,D,W}` + `div:Y` (s284/s285). Un-flattens the
  WHNF pole.

## Pre-registered gates (frozen before scoring; verbatim from the instrument)

| gate | statistic | null | predict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G1** effective-rank | PR(eigs) of the gram | `matched_range`(offdiag) | LESS |
| **G2** three-pole partition | block-contrast(fire/halt/div) | `shuffled_label`(node→cluster) | GREATER |
| **G3** eigvec↔partition | fire−halt contrast energy in top-3 eigenspace | `shuffled_label`(partition) | GREATER |
| **G4** spectral universality | mean pairwise cos of normalized spectra | `matched_range` per-model spectra | GREATER |
| **G5** φ-trap calibration | −\|λ₀/λ₁ − φ⁴ᐟ⁵\| (closeness) | `matched_range`(offdiag) | GREATER (**EXPECTED FAIL**) |

`α=0.05`, `n_iter=2000`, seed `20250804`, 11 models with both grams
(gemma-4-31b, olmo-2-13b, pythia-{14m,160m,2.8b,410m}, qwen3-{0.6b,4b,14b,32b},
qwen3.6-27b).

## Results

### G1 — the un-flattening is a spectral collapse, not just added detail

| | 9×9 PR (of 9) | G1 verdict | 17×17 PR (of 17) | G1 verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| range over 11 models | **5.79 – 7.23** | **FAIL** (p 0.15–0.41) | **2.57 – 3.15** | **PASS** (p=5×10⁻⁴, 11/11) |

- **The 9×9 is spectrally diffuse — near-full-rank.** Its eigenvalues sit near 1

  (distinct opcodes), and the spectrum confirms it.
- **The 17×17 is rank-3.** Effective rank ≈ 2.9 out of 17, far below the null
  (p=5×10⁻⁴ every model). Enormous eigengap: Qwen3-32B **8.52, 4.47, 0.93 → cliff

  (one halt pole) and the 9 crystal opcodes are near-collinear in outcome space
  (one firing pole) → the whole 17-node cloud lives in ~3 dimensions.

**The 9×9 measures opcode IDENTITY (near-orthogonal, high rank); the 17×17
measures reduction OUTCOME (three poles, low rank).** They are different views,
and the un-flattening reveals the low-dimensional outcome geometry the collapsed
WHNF node was hiding (the s284 G4 dissociation, now spectral).

### G2 — the three poles are real (11/11)

node-shuffle null in **11/11 models** (binomial p ≈ 0). Fire (crystal) ⊥ halt
(`whnf:X`); `div:Y` its own pole. The three-cluster geometry is a fact, not an
imposed partition.

### G3 — the poles ARE the dominant eigenstructure (11/11)

The fire−halt contrast vector's energy in the top-3 eigenspace beats the
shuffled-partition null in **11/11 models** (binomial p ≈ 0). The PR≈3 axes are
the semantic poles, not incidental variance.

### G4 — spectral-SHAPE universality is NOT established (honest null)

Normalized spectra are extremely alike across models (mean pairwise cos **0.992**
[9×9], **0.994** [17×17]) — but so are matched-range random-gram spectra, so the
gate **FAILS** (p ≈ 0.10 / 0.12). Sorted normalized spectra are intrinsically
similar; this statistic cannot separate real from chance. **Universality lives in
the relational off-diagonal pattern (C2), not in the eigenvalue profile.** A clean
```

### Thinking

I'm working through the mathematical structure here—the 9×9 matrix shows identity properties with near-orthogonal characteristics and high rank, where the real structure emerges in the sign patterns of off-diagonal elements. The 17×17 matrix represents a lower-dimensional outcome space with rank-3 geometry capturing three distinct poles. The core insight is that the topology of how information routes through these spaces matters more than the raw magnitudes themselves.

Michael's point crystallizes this: the system combines geometry and signals through soft topology routing, which is why our magnitude-based approaches failed. Now I'm considering whether these mathematical structures can actually guide the country-capital linking task. The honest answer involves a register mismatch—the 9×9 and 17×17 matrices describe opcode routing within the lambda-reduction system, not the factual multi-hop reasoning needed for country-capital pairs. Applying them directly as filters on country residuals would violate the domain separation, since they measure opcode-routing geometry rather than factual-reasoning geometry.

But the method itself transfers perfectly. The real insight from the gram analysis is that the low-rank routing topology is what matters—the magnitudes are just model-specific scaffolding that doesn't generalize. Our fast_plate construction failed because we reinjected the raw prototype with all its mixed geometry and signal together. The fix is to build a task-native gram from the country representations themselves, extract its low-rank routing structure to find the country-reasoning axes, then project the country embeddings onto that routing subspace while stripping away the magnitude scaffolding. This is the right way to use the gram as a filter: let the task define its own topology, then write in that topology-register instead of the magnitude-register.

The deeper question is whether this country-hop routing shares the same outcome geometry as the crystal's fire/halt/diverge structure—that would suggest factual composition and lambda reduction use a shared outcome-routing register. But that's speculative; I need to build the country-hop outcome gram, check if it's low-rank and fire/halt/diverge-like, and test whether it aligns with the crystal 17×17. This is a measurement problem, not an assumption.

For P-HHOP-WRITE, avenue 1 already captures what the h-hop reads through CAP_QUERY geometry. The gram approach upgrades this by projecting the reinject value onto the routing subspace instead of using raw population-centered directions—building the country gram at the capture layer, extracting its low-rank routing subspace, and projecting v_c onto it. This directly operationalizes Michael's "topology routing not magnitudes" principle on the construction side, unifying capture geometry with the gram filtering method.

This creates two parallel writes: hhop_plate (raw) versus hhop_routing_plate (gram-filtered). If the gram-filtered write succeeds where the raw doesn't, that validates the routing-register write as the missing ingredient—mirroring how s269/s303 validated the extraction side. I need guardrails for the low-rank projection risk: the routing subspace must outperform both a random subspace of matching rank and a shuffled country null, using the same validation discipline as the gram gates.

The gram filter is the principled projector for writing into the routing register—our three failures were all register/geometry misses, and this gives us the direct path. GD finds the soft topology routing by reshaping the band in the routing register, while a gram-filtered construction attempts to write that topology routing directly in closed form rather than letting GD discover it iteratively.

My recommendation is to fold a gram-routing filter into the P-HHOP-WRITE design: build the task-native country gram at the capture layer, project the reinject onto its low-rank routing subspace, and add it as a separate arm alongside the raw hhop arm. Then separately probe whether the factual hop shares the crystal's outcome geometry—measure the task's own grams rather than blindly using the crystal's.

### Thinking

I've completed the first task with the pre-registration frozen at commit 44b14f4. Now I'm moving into task 2, which involves extending fast_plate.py with several new components: adding the --reinject-geometry option with name, hhop, and hhop_routing variants, implementing the CAP_QUERY capture-layer scan, incorporating country gram analysis with eigengap ranking, setting up the routing projection, building the F4 subspace-real gate, and defining the new arms and verdicts. Given how much ground this covers, I need to map out the implementation strategy before diving in.

The key decision I'm wrestling with is how to structure the experiment modes. The s305 run used fixed arms (base/fast_plate/static/shuffle), but the P-HHOP-WRITE experiment needs different arms (base/hhop_routing/hhop_raw/static/shuffle) plus the capture scan and gram analysis. Rather than forking the code into separate paths, I should add an --experiment flag that branches within run_model, keeping the shared logic unified while letting the arms and verdicts adapt based on which experiment is active. I'm weighing whether to implement this as a separate function within fast_plate.py using a closure to share model handles, or create a dedicated script—the two-hook read≠write mechanism plus the gram eigengap and F4 subspace logic feels substantial enough to warrant its own structure, but refactoring for shared state might be overkill.

Looking at the project's existing patterns, routing_register.py is already a separate script that imports the shared wb module to reuse core machinery like gates and derangement logic rather than forking the code. Following that precedent, I should create a new hhop_write.py script that imports fast_plate and reuses the capture, scan, and gate infrastructure while adding the hhop-specific logic.

But the problem is that fast_plate's most useful pieces—capture_all and make_hook—are closures bound inside run_model, so they're not directly importable. I could refactor fast_plate to expose these at the module level, but that's a lot of work. Given the constraints, extending fast_plate.py itself makes more sense: I'll add the hhop path as a branch inside run_model controlled by a command-line flag, which lets me reuse all the closure-bound machinery without duplication.

The tricky part is that make_hook currently works with a single layer (L*), but for the read≠write case I need hooks at two different layers that share state. I should create a new helper that builds a pair of hooks for this scenario—one reading from L_read and one writing to L_write—while keeping them coordinated through a shared store.

For the CLI, I'll add an `--experiment` flag to choose between the baseline and the hhop-write variant, which will automatically run both the raw and routing arms. This is cleaner than having separate geometry flags. The issue is that a single `--reinject-geometry` value can't deliver both hhop_raw and hhop_routing in one run, so I'm pivoting to `--experiment {fast-plate, hhop-write}` where hhop-write handles all the geometry arms together. While this deviates from the frozen flag name, it's an implementation detail rather than a scientific parameter change, so it shouldn't violate the pre-registration.

Now I'm ready to implement the code by adding an `hhop-write` branch inside the run_model closure that gates on `args.experiment == "hhop-write"`, reusing the existing capture and evaluation machinery while adding the CAP scan, grammar, dual-hook reinject, and hhop scoring logic. I'm deciding to keep the hhop branch inline in run_model rather than factoring it out, since adding ~120 lines to an already ~250-line function is reasonable for a research tool. Now I need to implement the module-level helper that computes the country gram subspace by centering the data, computing the gram matrix, finding the eigengap, and extracting the top-k eigenvectors.

Actually, I'm realizing the gram matrix G = R R^T lives in country space (16×16), but I need projectors in feature space (D-dim). The key insight is that the right singular vectors of R give me the principal directions in feature space—so I should compute the SVD of R, use the singular values to identify the eigengap, and extract the top-k right singular vectors as my feature-space projector.

Now I'm implementing this: I'll normalize the rows of R to unit norm, compute the SVD, and use the singular values squared to determine k by looking for the gap. Then I can project centered country vectors onto the subspace spanned by those top-k right singular vectors.

Defining the function...

For the matched-rank random subspace baseline, I'll generate a random orthonormal matrix of the same dimensions and project onto that instead. And then there's the two-hook reinject strategy—recognizing the routing decision at layer L* and reinjecting the routed representation at L_cap.

The key insight is that both hooks need to share state about which token fired and what the routing score was. The read hook at L* computes the routing logits and stores the max score and fired token index in a shared dictionary, then the write hook at L_cap retrieves those values to reinject the representation. Since we're processing one forward pass at a time, a single shared store works fine—it just gets overwritten each forward.

For positions, the routing happens per token in the sequence, and we reinject at those same positions in the later layer, so everything stays aligned. If L_cap equals L*, we can use a single hook that does both recognition and reinjection in the same layer. Otherwise, I'll create a function that registers both hooks together and returns their handles.

Now I'm implementing the actual hook logic inside the model run — setting up a shared store to pass data between the read and write hooks, where the read hook extracts activations, computes which prototypes fire, and stores the routing decisions, then the write hook retrieves those decisions and adds the prototype deltas back into the hidden states.

I'm working through how to handle the static mode for this architecture, since unlike the previous approach where read and write happened at the same layer, here they're separated — the recognition happens at one layer but the reinjection needs to work across that boundary.

The key insight is to store both the hard routing information (which prototype fired) and the soft weights from the softmax, then let each write variant use what it needs. I'll structure the read hook to capture the projection matrix and compute the softmax weights, then the write hooks can either use the hard routing for fast variants or apply the soft weighted combination for the static mode. Adding the F4 arm with random subspace routing, then I need to implement the full hhop branch in fast_plate.py with helper functions for eigengap ranking, routing subspace selection, and scoring logic, plus a conditional branch in run_model to handle the experiment routing.

The tricky part is that run_hhop needs access to all the closures from run_model, so I'll need to inline the hhop arms directly rather than extract them into a separate function—that way they can access make_hook, run_arm, and the base results without passing everything through parameters. I need to add a variant of run_arm that handles two hooks for the hhop case, where one hook reads from L* and another writes to L_cap, both sharing the same store. I'll define these helper functions as closures inside run_model so they have access to the existing context, then read through the current end of run_model to see where to insert this branching logic.

```
def verdict_of(gate0_ok: bool, m_pass: bool, r: dict) -> str:
    if not gate0_ok:
        return "VOID (gate-0)"
    if not m_pass:
        return "STILL-EXTERNAL-BY-MEASUREMENT"
    if not r["F5"]:
        return "HOST-DAMAGED"
    if r["F1"] and r["F2"] and r["F3"]:
        return ("FAST-PLATE-WIRES (+COLLAPSE-LOAD-BEARING)" if r["collapse_sig"]
                else "FAST-PLATE-WIRES (+GEOMETRY-SUFFICES)")
    if r["F1"]:
        return "UNSPECIFIC"
    return "FAST-PLATE-INERT"
```

### Thinking

I need to fix a few linting errors from ruff, particularly the line-too-long issues around line 801. On the validation side, the spectral clustering is picking up k=2 when I need k=3, and the routing retention is only at 0.86. Looking at the planted eigenvalues [16, 4, 1], the largest eigengap should be between the first and second eigenvalues, but something's off with how the singular values are scaling relative to the gram matrix eigenvalues.

The real problem is that I unit-normalized the rows before computing the SVD, which flattens the variance structure and destroys the clean [16, 4, 1] eigenvalue pattern I was expecting. The gram matrix of unit-normalized rows doesn't preserve that scaling, especially since the cosine gram approach (which uses unit-normalized vectors) was designed for a different purpose than capturing the subspace structure I need for routing. The planted test with unit-normalized coefficients should theoretically show three equal eigenvalues from the isotropic 3D subspace, creating a clear gap at e3 and identifying k=3. But the actual result is k=2 with 0.86 retained, suggesting the noise is inflating e3 or the [4,2,1] magnitude weighting is making the smallest-weight direction comparable to noise, so the eigengap appears earlier in the ratio sequence. I should switch to isotropic coefficients with equal weights across all three dimensions and reduce the noise to create a cleaner rank-3 signal with a sharp eigenvalue drop. This way the gram matrix will have three comparable eigenvalues followed by a large gap to the noise floor, making k=3 the correct answer. I also need to ensure retain_routing stays above 0.9 so the routing subspace properly captures the sign information. I'll keep the unit-normalization for consistency with the crystal gram approach and the angular principal directions. Now I need to fix the ruff linting errors and update the planted data test to use isotropic coefficients.

### Thinking

The end-to-end smoke test ran successfully—the CAP scan executed with expected parameters, the gram computation identified the eigengap structure, and the capital alignment metrics came back low as intended, confirming it's not just doing a simple lookup. All five arms plus the shuffle variant completed, and the scoring and verdict systems are functioning properly, though it looks like the thought got cut off mid-sentence.

Now I'm noticing something concerning: in this two-cell smoke test, reinject_landed is exactly 0.000 and all arms match the baseline exactly, which feels like noise rather than a real directional signal. But here's what's bothering me—in the fast-plate smoke test, reinject_landed was 0.062 (nonzero, indicating the hook fired), yet here it's precisely 0.000. That suggests the two-hook read≠write mechanism might not be applying the delta correctly. Both hooks are registered on the same layer (dec[24]), with the read hook firing first to store the layer output, then the write hook firing second to add the delta using that stored value. The order should be correct, but the exact zero is suspicious—it might mean the write hook isn't actually modifying anything when both hooks target the same layer.

The real puzzle is that getting exactly 0.000 across all arms (raw, routing, randsub, shuffle) feels too coincidental to be orthogonality. The fast-plate test used the NAME prototype scaled by S=1.185 and got 0.062, while this hhop test uses the CAP geometry vectors at the same strength but gets nothing. I need to verify whether `store["P"]` is actually available when the write hook fires, since both hooks are on the same module and registration order determines execution order. The read_hook and write_hook split across two layers should be functionally equivalent to fast-plate's single hook when L_cap equals L*, except the proto vector differs. The exact 0.000 delta is suspicious though — I need to check the smoke results to see if all deltas are truly zero (indicating the hook isn't firing) or just rounded down from something tiny.

## Two heads-up from the scan (frozen-procedure output, not held-split direction)
1. **`L_cap = 24`** — the capture scan co-located the write layer with L\* (it searches L ≥ L\*, and `country_present − capital_leak` peaked at 24). So the read≠write separation didn't engage this run; it's testing *geometry* (CAP vs name) at L24, honestly chosen by the frozen rule.
2. **`capital_leak = 0.62` at L24** — on the clean country prompt, ~62% of capitals are already the argmax by L24, so the h-hop largely completes early. That's a real **lookup risk** — but `cos(v_raw, capital) = 0.138` is low (population-centering strips the capital component), so it's not capital-like by geometry. **F2 is the arbiter.**
3. **`gram_k = 2`** — the country routing subspace at L24 is rank-2 (its own eigengap, not forced).

None of these are defects; they're exactly the quantities the run adjudicates. Write is register-matched (weak, ~0.1 logit shifts, as s305) — `reinject_landed` + `routing_advantage` will attribute the verdict.

### Thinking

The test run is complete with a verdict of HHOP-INERT. Looking at the results, most flags are disabled except F5, and the routing advantage shows only a marginal improvement of 0.026 that doesn't reach statistical significance—the routing performance barely edges out the baseline. The reinject metric is quite weak at 0.033, and I'm seeing parameters like a co-located L_cap of 24 and a low cosine capital value of 0.138.

I just realized there's a critical issue: the script ran without an explicit `--out` flag, so it defaulted to writing results.json to the fast-plate directory instead of the hhop-write directory where the run log went. This means the new results have overwritten the previously committed s305 fast-plate results—that's a mistake I need to address.

The good news is the s305 results are still in git history, so I can recover them. My plan is to first copy the current (hhop-write) results to the correct location, then restore the original s305 results.json from git. The routing filter didn't produce meaningful gains—routing_advantage only +0.026, so the topology approach isn't helping linearly. This confirms the HHOP-INERT finding: routing is soft and nonlinear, shaped primarily by gradient descent rather than hard wiring. It's not a dead end though; there are still other paths to explore like read≠write dynamics or distributed writes. Interestingly, L_cap=24 handles most of the h-hop work on clean prompts with capital_leak=0.62, but the scan couldn't locate a country-not-capital layer at that depth.

Now I'm seeing the real timing issue: on landmark prompts, the country only crystallizes at L24, but on clean prompts the capital's already formed by L24. So when the landmark-inferred country finally materializes, the h-hop window (which runs ≤24 on clean data) has already closed—the intermediate arrives too late for the circuit to use it. This is a sharp mechanistic explanation for the one-shot composition failure, complementary to the s300 nonlinearity finding. The scheduling mismatch explains why the composition breaks.

I need to fix the file location issue first, then pull the full results with exact numbers and synthesize everything.

### Thinking

Looking at the HHOP-INERT results, the key finding is that the gram routing filter didn't provide a meaningful advantage over the raw approach—the routing benefit was only +0.026 with p=0.491, which isn't statistically significant. The F1 scores show B1 performing perfectly while B2 is essentially equivalent to the base model despite a small positional shift, and the cosine similarity for gram_k=2 is quite low at 0.138.

The critical insight comes from the capital_scan analysis: country tokens are trivially present at every layer, but capital_leak reveals the real story—it starts at 0.62 by L24 (the earliest candidate layer) and rises monotonically to 1.0 by L33-35. This means the h-hop computation for country→capital is already 62% complete by the time the landmark-inferred country materializes at L24, creating a timing mismatch where the country information arrives too late for the model to effectively use it in the h-hop pathway. So the gram routing filter didn't actually solve the problem because the core issue is timing, not geometry — the country arrives too late for the h-hop to use it, and no amount of geometric routing can fix that. This sharpens the picture: decodability and usability are separate (s305), but the real blocker is phase mismatch (s306). The fix needs to ensure the g-hop completes early enough, or the country gets re-encoded fresh for the h-hop to consume. Let me verify the timing more carefully. The capital_leak metric at L24 shows 62% completion on the CAP_QUERY prompt where the country token is present early, but on the DIRECT prompt the country hasn't materialized yet—it only emerges at L24. So injecting it there gives layers beyond 24 the chance to complete the h-hop, and indeed layers 24-35 push that 0.62 completion all the way to 1.0, meaning the h-hop does finish within the available depth.

But then why does injecting the country at L24 on DIRECT fail to produce the capital? Layers 25-35 clearly have the capacity to complete the h-hop since they do exactly that on CAP_QUERY, yet the routing stays inert with B2 at 0.591, barely above the baseline. The timing story alone doesn't explain this—there must be something else about how the injected country direction differs from the naturally-emerged one.

The injected country vector from CAP_QUERY's L24 might not be in the form that layers 25-35 actually read. Or more likely, at L24 on CAP_QUERY the representation is already a mixture—62% capital already formed plus country information—so when I center the per-country residuals, I'm capturing country plus partial-capital, which explains why the capital cosine is only 0.138 after centering. The real issue might be that the write strength is too weak: at 0.033 reinject_landed, the injected country is a negligible perturbation against the DIRECT residual, which already contains its own latent (and incorrect) content at L24.

Beyond that, the DIRECT prompt's residual at L24 isn't a clean slate for the h-hop to work with—it's "...where it is located is" with country information buried but not materialized in the format the h-hop needs. Injecting a country direction doesn't automatically reshape it into "capital of {country} is" structure. So it's a combination of weak write strength plus a format and context mismatch. Even though layers 25-35 still have h-hop capacity, the one-shot linker doesn't form because the injection is too faint relative to the existing residual noise, and the input context isn't in the right shape for the h-hop to latch onto.

The core findings are clear: writing the measured h-hop country geometry at L24—whether raw or filtered through gram routing—doesn't establish the wire (routing B2 ≈ 0.591 versus base 0.545, not significant). The gram routing filter provided no advantage either, so the failure isn't a register mismatch we can fix through projection. And it's not a lookup problem either, since the arm didn't beat baseline in the first place.

The weak write persists even after reinjection, and the scan reveals that capital_leak is already saturated at L24 (0.62, climbing to 1.0), meaning the h-hop and landmark-country materialization overlap in depth—a timing observation that's suggestive but doesn't prove a hard wall. Since the routing-register write didn't rescue the failure, the problem runs deeper than just geometry-register misalignment; it's a combination of weak write, soft nonlinear routing, and this depth-overlap timing dynamic.

Re-encoding with chain-of-thought resets the phase, which aligns with the s295 exhaustion law under this depth-timing constraint. The honest takeaway is that this construction—with measured h-hop geometry and gram-routing filtering—doesn't work here, but that doesn't invalidate the routing thesis; it just means this particular failure isn't the register the filter was designed to address. The next moves are either pushing the g-hop earlier or building an in-forward re-encoding relay.

Now I'm committing the script fix (real fix, error to success), then the hhop results, then drafting the reframed §Result that frames this as a timing insight without claiming closure. After that I'll update the session state and prepare the batch for Michael's approval—he asked me to skip memory last time, so I'll draft without it and offer it separately if needed.

```
SuperBake: Installing Verified Facts into Transformer
Weights by Direct Construction
Albert Ruehlman
AMI Labs
howchunconsulting@gmail.com
July 22, 2026
Abstract
We present SuperBake, a system that installs new factual knowledge into the weights of
large language models without any gradient steps . Instead of fine-tuning, low-rank adaptation,
or retrieval, SuperBake measures how the stock model represents each question and then hand-
constructs a small circuit of neurons — exact weights, written directly — in a region appended
to the network’s MLPs. Each installed fact receives a physical address (layer and slot coordi-
nates), is behaviorally verified across phrasing variants, and is neutralized (zeroed) if verification
fails, so delivered weights never carry silently broken knowledge. The output is a standard check-
point that stock inference code loads unchanged, together with a receipt listing every fact, its
verification status, and its coordinates.
On a 7,450-row battery over 1,000 facts (Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct), the constructed engine veri-
fies 91.3% of rows overall against 76.9% for a strong masked-SGD baseline — while leaving prose
perplexity at the stock baseline, where the SGD baseline more than doubles it. Constructed
facts answer reverse questions at ∼94% where the trained baseline scores 0.7%: the reversal
curse does not apply when the reverse mapping is simply written. Delivered, stock-loadable
checkpoints reach 93–97% primary-question recall on Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-7B. Construc-
tion is fast: measured end-to-end bakes run in minutes on one 80 GB GPU, and a reduced
pipeline wrote 192 facts into a raw Pythia-6.9B at 14.8 facts per second.
The method was derived from a mechanistic post-mortem of what stochastic gradient descent
actually builds when it crams facts into appended neurons. We report those findings: dense fact
storage lands in a coherent sub-threshold population code living in activation-function leakage
— the same signal that damages prose — and several transport and capacity laws that any
weight-space editor must respect. SuperBake is the constructive rebuild of that mechanism: the
same associative memory, but gated, addressable, verifiable, and harmless to the host model.
1 Introduction
Getting new knowledge into a language model’s weights is still surprisingly hard. Fine-tuning on
new facts damages what the model already knows and, at practical fact densities, its general prose
ability; parameter-eﬀicient variants inherit the same interference because they optimize the same
objective. Retrieval-augmented generation sidesteps the problem by not writing the weights at
all — the knowledge lives in an external index, with its latency, context cost, and failure modes.
Knowledge-editing methods such as ROME and MEMIT [ 2, 3] write weights directly, but they
modify existing projections via least-squares updates, are evaluated largely on single-hop cloze
recall, and recent lifelong-editing evaluations at realistic scale find they fall behind simple retrieval
or continued fine-tuning [ 8].
1
This paper describes a different route. SuperBake treats a fact as a small circuit to be engineered,
not a gradient target or a database row. The system (1) appends fresh, initially-silent neuron slots
to the model’s MLPs, leaving every original weight untouched; (2) measures how the stock model
represents each question across phrasings and contexts; (3) writes exact weights for a per-fact circuit
— recognition keys, an injected code, answer readouts, spelling chains, and delivery insurance —
derived in closed form from those measurements; (4) calibrates the result in a measure-and-correct
loop that uses no loss function and no gradients; and (5) verifies every fact behaviorally, zeroing
the neurons of any fact that fails, before saving a standard checkpoint.
Because every weight is written deliberately, each fact has a legible anatomy: a receipt lists the
exact layer-and-slot coordinates of its neurons. Ablate them and the fact is gone; leave them and
it survives subsequent fine-tuning better than the model’s own pretrained knowledge (§ 4). Because
nothing is optimized against the model’s existing behavior, the host is unharmed: across our main
runs the prose negative log-likelihood of baked models is equal to — in several runs slightly better
than — the stock baseline.
Contributions.
1. A zero-gradient installation method for factual knowledge in transformer LLMs: per-fact
constructed circuits in appended MLP slots, closed-form keys and values, and a gradient-free
closed-loop calibrator, delivered as bone-stock checkpoints (§ 3).
2. A verification-first pipeline : phrasing batteries, held-knowledge and fluency referees, and
a pruner that guarantees verified or neutralized — failed facts degrade to honest ignorance,
never to garbage (§ 3.9).
3. Results (§4): 91.3% vs. 76.9% overall against a strong masked-SGD baseline at zero prose
cost; delivered checkpoints at 93–97%; dissolution of the reversal curse by construction ( ∼94%
vs. 0.7%); durability under further fine-tuning; minutes-scale bakes.
4. Mechanistic findings (§5): measured evidence that SGD stores dense fact overflow in a
coherent sub-threshold population code carried by activation leakage; transport and capacity
laws for signals injected into a transformer’s residual stream; and a compendium of design
laws (§ 7) for weight-space engineering.
5. Composition by construction (§3.8): one additional constructed neuron per fact gives
installed facts silent multi-hop behavior (using a fact without being asked for it), exceeding
what emerges natively at comparable scale.
We close with an honest boundary (§ 8): free-flowing conversational recall on the bare weights
— pronouns, ellipsis, novel context mid-dialogue — is measurably harder than any enumerated
battery and remains an open frontier; delivered models disclose exactly which phrasings verified
conversationally.
2 Background and Notation
Four standard mechanistic-interpretability facts carry the whole method; readers fluent in the
circuits literature can skim.
2
The residual stream is a shared bus. A decoder block reads its input from, and adds its
output to, a per-token vector stream xℓ ∈ Rd [9]. Anything a layer writes is visible to every later
layer. A constructed neuron at layer 6 can therefore deposit a message that a constructed reader
at layer 25 consumes.
MLP neurons are keyed value-writers. In a SwiGLU MLP the i-th neuron computes
outi = silu( gi · x)| {z }
gate
(ui · x)| {z }
up
vi|{z}
value
, (1)
a two-factor match on the current state followed by the addition of a fixed direction vi to the
stream [ 1]. Writing a neuron by hand means choosing a key (what it fires on), a second factor
(an AND condition), and a value (what it writes). Pythia-style MLPs lack the up path and carry
biases; the construction adapts (§ 6).
Attention heads are transport. A head moves information between positions: QK decides
from where, OV decides what is carried [ 9]. Under rotary position embeddings [ 16], the QK match
acquires a distance-dependent kernel set by which rotary frequencies the key/query directions oc-
cupy — a fact we exploit (§ 3.6) and are limited by (§ 7).
Directions read out as token preferences. Projecting a residual state onto unembedding
rows scores next-token candidates (the “logit lens” [ 11]); the logit gap between the correct answer
token and its strongest competitor at the answer position is the scalar our calibrator drives positive.
That is the entire theoretical load. Everything else in the paper is measurement and construc-
tion.
3 The SuperBake Engine
3.1 Appended capacity and stock delivery
Before any writing, every MLP in the model is expanded by the same number E of zero columns
and rows — new neurons whose weights are exactly zero, hence perfectly silent. Uniform expansion
matters: the delivered config declares one intermediate_size, so the checkpoint loads in stock
transformers/vLLM with no custom code. Facts are then written into the delivered geometry : the
states we measure during construction are the states the customer’s copy will compute. (An early
pipeline that compacted and re-ordered columns at save time taught us this the hard way: reorder-
ing thousands of columns changes bf16 accumulation order and flips borderline facts; building in
final geometry removes the gap. Save/reload round-trips are exact — verified to four decimals of
NLL and row-for-row recall.) Unused slots cost disk, so dead neurons are pruned to zero and the
expansion is sized from the battery ( ∼0.5 GB per 1,000 slots per layer at 8B scale).
3.2 Harvest: photographing the stock model
All keys and thresholds are derived from measured states. The engine runs the unmodified model
over every question rendered several ways — phrasing variants, casing variants, chat-template and
plain renders, and several lightings: different batch compositions and conversational prefixes that
perturb the states the way deployment will. It also harvests innocents: states from generic prose,
ordinary chat, and held-out known-fact questions, which become the negative population every

Figure 1: Anatomy of one installed fact on Llama-3.1-8B (32 layers). Each site is a small bank of hand-

them is what makes keys transfer out of the build context (§ 6).
3.3 Code keys at layer 6
Each fact f gets one recognition neuron low in the stack. Its key is a Mahalanobis matched filter
on the question-end state,
kf = Σ −1 (¯xf − µ), (2)
where ¯xf is the mean state over fact f ’s renders and (µ, Σ) are estimated over the union population
— all facts’ states and the innocents. Whitening is mandatory in crowded key space: raw mean
keys mostly measure the shared question subspace (own-fact response 0.82 vs. strongest other 4.87
before whitening), and excluding innocents from Σ lets the inverse amplify them. The threshold
is set above every other fact’s response and every innocent’s, with a hard clearance floor. The
neuron’s value writes a random unit vector cf — the fact’s code — into the stream, scaled to a
target arrival magnitude.
Two properties do the work. First, the matched filter generalizes over phrasing: any rendering
of the question lands near ¯xf after whitening. Second, the injected code manufactures separability
4
downstream: at layer 25, raw question states of different facts overlap badly (median nearest-
neighbor margin ∼1.1), but with codes present every fact is linearly separable (4,750/4,750 at
1,000-fact scale, median margin 5.8). The code is the address the rest of the circuit keys on.
Born-hard gates. Hand-set thresholds sit in silu’s leaky region, and thousands of sub-threshold
leaks sum into prose damage (§ 5). Every constructed gate is therefore born hard : scale the gate
row and bias by s and divide the value column by s; silu(s z)/s approximates a hard threshold with
leak ≤ 0.28/s, exponentially dead where prose states sit. At s=8–16 constructed banks contribute
exactly zero measured prose drift.
3.4 Readout and chains at layer 25
The speaking end is a per-fact readout neuron and a bank of chain neurons. The readout’s gate
keys on the code-enriched question state; its up path is a question-mode ramp — a direction that
separates “a question is being answered here” from prose, making the neuron a multiplicative AND
(Eq. 1) that cannot fire during ordinary text. Its value pushes the answer’s first token through the
unembedding and suppresses the measured competitor : the highest-logit wrong token observed for
that fact, with the suppression direction orthogonalized against the answer direction (correlated
unembeddings otherwise make suppression drag the answer down with it).
Answers longer than one token are carried by per-transition chain neurons — one per (fact,
answer-position) — keyed on the mid-answer state and pushing the next token. Three structural
rules were each forced by a measured failure: (i) keys are fully per-row, never group-mean (a group-
mean key thresholds at its worst phrasing and overshoots its siblings); (ii) rows of the same fact
are allies, excluded from one another’s negative pools (a sibling cross-fire pushes the same token
— harmless); (iii) siblings that share a transition each carry 1/n of the push, because generation
sums them — eleven same-fact rows each sized to close the full gap once blew the residual out of
distribution and produced junk argmaxes.
3.5 The closed loop: calibration without gradients
Construction gets facts near correct; a measurement loop finishes them. For each fact the engine
renders the question, runs the model, and reads the logit gap at the answer position. Failing
facts get their own value column rescaled — nobody else’s weights move. The step size uses a
secant estimate of transfer: a push written at layer 25 traverses six blocks and a final RMSNorm,
arriving attenuated by a per-fact factor ˆf ≈ 0.2–0.5; measuring (closed gap)/(intended push) and
dividing the next intent by ˆf converges in a few rounds. Guard rails, each added after a measured
pathology: a backfire guard (a fact that regresses under a boost gets its column shrunk instead —
pure ˆf adaptation spirals); a two-backfire freeze; multi-token suppression (the last stubborn facts
were losing to prefix-fragment tokenizations of their own answers, e.g. ' Le' for Le Havre — the top
four wrong tokens are suppressed together); and front-to-back chain repair (transition k lives inside
the context of k+1, so only the earliest failing transition advances per round; unordered repair
oscillates). Chain repairs are confirmed against the verifier’s exact render, and readout repair runs
before chain repair — a readout push at the question position propagates through attention into
mid-answer states and un-wins transitions repaired first.
3.6 Transport: the attention organ
Everything above keys on states at the question’s end — which works until the question lives
deep in a conversation, where transcript context shifts those states off the keys. The stable signal,
5
measured across depths, is the state at the subject’s own tokens (±0.3 units across six turns, versus
30+ for question-end states). What is missing is transport: that identity must reach the generation
position. Native heads do not reliably do it for novel entities, so the engine constructs a head .
A donor-head scan finds attention groups whose ablation does not hurt (often slightly improves)
prose; the organ overwrites one at layer 24. Its QK are rank-one outer products: Wq = a c ˆµ⊤
(queries fire from every position along a carrier direction c), Wk = b c g⊤ (g a subject-token direction,
so keys are large only at subject mentions). The carrier’s support on the rotary spectrum shapes
the kernel : slow dimensions give a flat any-distance floor; a mid band (periods of roughly 60–250
tokens) adds constructive interference only at small relative distance — recency selection. At true
conversation replays the head places 0.55–0.88 of its attention mass on the correct subject’s tokens,
resolving pronouns by discourse recency with zero additional parameters. Per-subject value lanes
write orthonormal payload directions (chosen in the low-variance subspace of the residual, SNR
≈ 13 vs. random directions) that downstream readout gates consume one layer later — writing
at layer 24 for layer-25 readers because payloads do not survive many blocks at readable strength
(§7). Selector directions are orthogonalized across subjects; before that fix, shared person-name
components caused cross-subject leakage.
3.7 Delivery insurance at the last layer
Some contexts fight back: for peak-prior continuations the native circuitry above layer 25 amplifies
against an injected push — boosting the answer raised its competitor faster. The engine’s answer
is positional: a small rescue bank at layer 31, one RMSNorm from the logits, where no native
layer remains to respond. Rescue neurons key on the exact failing context state, with generic-
question and prose negatives, and carry orthogonalized answer-minus-competitor-centroid values
sized by the measured gap. The same site hosts the denial gag : instruct models carry a reflex that
answers unknown-person questions with “I couldn’t find information on…”; one neuron keyed on
the question-mode ramp subtracts the denial-opener direction, activation-normalized on delivered
states (a mis-normalized first attempt over-suppressed 2.5× and produced attractor junk — the
referee caught it).
3.8 Composition: hop enrichment
A fact you can only recite is weaker than a fact you can use. One further neuron per fact, placed
at 0.16× depth (layer 5 on Llama-8B — the band where native subject enrichment lives), keys on
the subject’s state and adds the answer entity’s own representation to the stream: after “ Marcus
lives in Lyon ” is installed, the subject’s tokens carry Lyon’s representation, and the model’s native
machinery can answer “ what continent does Marcus live on? ” without the fact ever being stated.
Attribution A/B on delivered checkpoints: relations never baked (continent, language of city of
residence) go from 2/12 and 0/12 without enrichment to 11/12 and 11/12 with it — above the
∼6/10 rate at which a 6.9B model composes its own native facts silently. An ordering law governs
the stage: enrichment must be written before every key harvest, because the engine must key on
the model it is delivering — retrofitting it after harvests broke canonical recall entirely.
3.9 Verification, the pruner, and the receipt
Every bake ends in an examination the model must pass as delivered :
• Battery: generated phrasing variants per fact — primary, casing, alias, profile (“tell me
about X”), and reverse (answer→subject) rows; teacher-forced checks are confirmed by greedy
6
Figure 2: A receipt rendered as a map: every installed fact’s neurons at their layer/slot coordinates in one
real bake. The public demo at albertmi.ai lets visitors click any of 500 Wikipedia facts and fly to its neurons.
generation, which catches chain deaths that teacher-forcing hides.
• Referees: held-out known-fact questions (the model must still know what it knew), prose
NLL on held-out text, and chat NLL on a held-out transcript — run after every engine stage
with automatic revert of any stage that regresses them; plus a plain-recall spot check, added
after a stage once passed every global referee while silently breaking per-row recall.
• The pruner : every row that fails verification is neutralized — key, bias, up row, and value
column zeroed (shared code neurons only when all their readers failed) — and the battery
re-runs. Failed facts degrade to the stock model’s honest ignorance. A broken row left in the
weights is worse than no row: it produces garbage near its key. Failed ̸= absent is a delivery
invariant.
• The receipt : a JSON artifact listing every fact, per-category verification results, referee
outcomes, and the layer/slot coordinates of every neuron of every fact (Fig. 2). Claims about
borderline counts use best-of- N confirm runs; GPU nondeterminism alone moves borderline
tallies by ±5–9 rows per 300.
4 Results
Against a strong trained baseline. The comparison target is not naive fine-tuning (which
destroys the host model) but the best gradient-based system we could build: SGD confined by
hard gradient masks to the same appended slots, with mixed batches, per-step guards, and an
audit that reverts harmful writes. Table 1 and Fig. 3: construction wins overall (91.3% vs. 76.9%)
and on every variant category, while the baseline’s recall is bought with a 2.97 → 7.24 prose NLL
collapse and construction’s is free. The reversal-curse row deserves emphasis: models trained on “ A

Durability and specificity . Under continued fine-tuning at sane learning rates, installed facts
persist without protection; under deliberately abusive fine-tuning, constructed facts outlive the
model’s own pretrained knowledge . A 1,200-row twin-stress battery (300 subjects × 4 near-identical
facts — the classic bleed geometry) verifies at 91.7% on Llama with zero measured twin-bleed (0/25
probes): whitened per-fact keys separate same-subject facts on relation words alone. Nonce installs
work: a model that has never seen the token sequence “Zarvantis” learns to spell and use it (4/4).
Speed and scale. Construction is measurement-bound, not optimization-bound. The production
engine bakes and fully verifies 1,000 facts in ∼14 minutes on one GPU. An earlier, reduced pipeline
(recognition + naming neurons only, on raw Pythia-6.9B) wrote 192 facts in 13.0 seconds — 14.8
facts/second — with zero interference at that scale, and reached 2,000 facts at 92.0% verified in
139 seconds with known-fact referees clean and prose NLL slightly improved. On WikiBigEdit [ 8]
real-world update questions, a 1,000-fact sample verifies at 859/1,000 — exceeding the 719 our best
gradient-based system managed on the same sample at roughly 700× the compute.
The public artifact. The pipeline runs as a self-serve service ( albertmi.ai): visitors submit facts,
watch the bake, chat with the result, and download the checkpoint with its receipt from Hugging
Face. A 500-fact Wikipedia atlas (Fig. 2) serves as a standing, inspectable demo bake.
5 What SGD Actually Builds
SuperBake’s design was not guessed; it was copied from a mechanism we first had to discover.
When the strong SGD baseline crams ∼1,000 facts into appended slots, the entire weight delta is
observable: we own the seed state, the gradient mask confines every update to known coordinates,
and the post state can be dumped and dissected. The post-mortem overturned our assumptions
three times, and each reversal is load-bearing for the final method.
No neuron contains its fact. Across 3,787 trained neurons, the trained value column’s top
pushed token is the fact’s own answer in under 0.3% of cases; per-neuron statistics of neurons
whose facts came alive versus stayed dead are indistinguishable. Storage is collective. Pools compose

super-additively (early pool alone +17.9 answer logits, late pool alone +4.3, together +30.4), and
the early deposit acts as a selectivity field : with it, exactly one of 207 candidate late neurons fires
per question; without it, a cross-fire soup.
The facts live in the leak. The decisive experiment (Fig. 4): sharpening every trained gate
into a hard threshold — a transformation that leaves supra-threshold behavior untouched and only
removes silu’s sub-threshold leak — fully heals prose ( 6.80 → 2.69 NLL, stock = 2.64) and collapses
verification from 270/300 to 3/300. Dense fact storage under SGD is a coherent analog population
code carried by activation leakage : thousands of neurons each responding below threshold, their
coherent sum encoding the answers. This explains, at once, why no column reads out an answer,
why alive and dead neurons look identical, why removing the single shared “answer-mode” direction
from all columns repairs prose and kills half the facts (one direction is simultaneously the prose
poison and the storage medium), and why the prose tax grows with stored volume — the medium
operates on prose too. It also sets a capacity law: allowed to hum, the analog code holds ∼9×
more facts than the same slots restricted to firing neurons under key crowding.
T ransport laws. Hand-written cross-layer signals fail in specific, measurable ways: directions
chosen in quiet subspaces (to be readable over natural traﬀic) are attenuated ∼30× by the in-
tervening network; loud directions travel with gain 1.9 but arrive rotated into shared, crowded
channels. SGD’s advantage is co-training both ends through the real Jacobian. The constructed
answer is to stop fighting the medium: make separability manufactured (injected codes, § 3.3) rather
than found, and read every payload one layer after it is written (§ 3.6).
The derived rule, then the ladder. Functionally, the trained solution is a mode-gated linear
associative memory operating in the leak zone — and Eq. 1 shows the MLP can express exactly

trained gate — which changes nothing above threshold and only kills sub-threshold leakage — returns prose

replacement was that literal solve. It plateaued at ∼58% — a single-layer linear map fights with only
the layers above it, while SGD’s early deposits ride nineteen native nonlinear layers of amplification;
the network is the kernel, and it is upstream . Capacity comes from many thresholded keys, not matrix
rank. Rebuilding along those two lines — per-fact hard-keyed neurons at both ends plus the closed
loop of § 3.5 — produced the ladder of Fig. 5: ten design rungs from 57.7% to 96.2%, past the SGD
reference, at exactly zero prose cost. Every rung was forced by a measured failure, and the five
constructed attempts that precededthe discovery of the leak substrate all failed for the same reason
in different costumes: they wrote digital circuits into what is natively an analog medium.
6 Cross-Architecture Portability
The recipe is stated in terms any decoder transformer exposes — states, unembeddings, MLP slots
— but each family taught a law.
Qwen2.5 (no MLP biases). Without biases there is nowhere to put a threshold. Folding
thresholds onto the residual stream’s constant carrier direction fails at exactly the positions that
matter: the carrier is a phantom at the tail (attention-sink and first-token positions carry ≈0 carrier
projection, evaporating any folded threshold). The fix is structural: project keys perpendicular to
the carrier and let the quadratic gate×up form supply selectivity — own-fact states score ∼6.5
against prose ≤ 0.3, and the square makes the leak ratio (0.3/6.5)2 ≈ 0.2%. Threshold-free, bias-
free, and the delivered checkpoint is the best of all models (97.3%/95.5%).
Pythia (GPT-NeoX, thin margins). Pythia exposed the sharpest law (Fig. 6): born-hard
banks amplify composition wobble . Cross-context state jitter of ±0.2 units is harmless at Llama’s
margins ( ∼7) and catastrophic at Pythia’s ( ∼0.9–2.6): a flipped near-threshold neuron is a flipped
8–28 logit push, so a calibration loop that measures in one batch composition converges to a mirage
— gaps closed in its own lighting, absent in truth. The cure is the multi-lighting harvest of § 3 taken

7 Design Laws
Each law in Table 2 was paid for with a refuted design; together they are the field manual this
paper exists to publish.
8 Limitations and the Honest Boundary
F ree conversation on bare weights. The strongest honest claim is scoped: installed facts
answer any baked phrasing, including mid-conversation (96–98% transcript-render recall), and para-
phrase generalizes. But fully free recall — pronouns, ellipsis, novel phrasings deep in a dialogue
whose history the model itself generated — is measurably harder: on a 20-turn adversarial no-crutch
battery the delivered weights answer 5–8/20, and four independent non-transport interventions each
cap at that band. The measured causes are distributional (self-generated conversational states are
not covered by any scripted harvest — the bench/battery divergence is itself the measurement)
and mechanical (delivery keys go key-dead at depth; the attention organ of § 3.6 is the constructed
response and has produced the first depth hits, e.g. pronoun follow-ups, while raising in-battery
verification to its all-time high). Delivered models disclose verified_conversational per fact in
the receipt, and ship with a small client-side phrasing-rescue script; the serving layer never answers
from the receipt.
Other boundaries. Answers that begin with the subject’s own name stress chain keys (an engine
fix is queued); one subject cannot carry two facts phrased as the same question (the augmenter

9 Related Work
Knowledge editing. ROME and MEMIT [2, 3] edit existing MLP projections via rank-constrained
least squares; MEND [ 4] and KE [ 5] learn editor networks; [ 6] fine-tunes with constraints. Su-
perBake differs in substrate (appended, initially-silent slots; originals untouched), in mechanism
(closed-form construction plus gradient-free calibration), in contract (behavioral verification with
neutralization and receipts), and in scope (multi-token answers, variants, reverse rows, conversa-
tion renders, composition). WikiBigEdit [ 8] finds editing methods underperforming retrieval and
fine-tuning at lifelong scale — consistent with our reading that editing existing weights competes
for shared capacity, which appending avoids. MLPs as memories. Our construction is a literal
engineering of the key–value view of transformer MLPs [ 1] and of classical associative memories [ 12];
knowledge-neuron attribution [ 7] similarly localizes facts in MLPs. Mechanistic interpretabil-
ity .The circuit formalism [ 9], logit-lens readouts [ 11], and superposition [ 10] supply the vocabulary;
our leak substrate (§ 5) is a superposed analog code, and our instruments are standard MI practice
turned into engineering referees. Alternatives. RAG [ 14] leaves weights unchanged; LoRA [ 15]
trains low-rank deltas with SGD’s interference profile. The reversal curse [ 13] motivates our reverse
rows. Model families: Llama 3 [ 18], Qwen2.5 [ 19], Pythia [ 17], RoPE [ 16].
12
Law Statement
Leak substrate Dense trained fact storage is a coherent sub-threshold population code
in activation leakage; prose damage and storage are the same signal
(Fig. 4).
Capacity Recall capacity scales with the number of thresholded keys, not matrix
rank; shared linear maps saturate near the hidden dimension.
Transport Quiet directions attenuate ∼30×; loud ones arrive rotated into shared
channels. Manufacture separability (codes); never rely on found chan-
nels.
Payload survival Read a constructed payload at the layer where it is written +1; it
does not survive many blocks at readable strength.
Whitening Matched-filter keys need union covariance (facts + innocents); raw
mean keys measure the shared question subspace.
Born-hard Every hand-set gate must be hardened ( ×s, /s); protection lives in
gates with clearance floors, never in solve-side zero targets.
Per-row keys Group-mean keys are diluted: thresholded at the worst phrasing, over-
sized for the rest. One row, one key.
Sibling sum Same-transition siblings sum at generation; each carries 1/n of the
push.
Ordering Later writes shift earlier measurements: enrichment before harvests;
readout repair before chain repair; earliest failing transition first.
Context binding Keys harvested under one context distribution exist only there when
margins are thinner than state wobble; harvest under many lightings,
threshold at worst case, verify under fresh ones.
RoPE locality Rank-one rotary QK kernels peak only at distance zero and oscillate
elsewhere: constructed heads cannot implement local windows; con-
tent selectivity must carry them.
Referee completeness Global referees (knowns, prose, chat) are blind to per-row damage;
every stage needs a plain-recall spot check.
Delivery honesty Failed ̸= absent: neutralize unverified rows; never let a serving layer
answer for the weights.
Confirm runs GPU nondeterminism moves borderline tallies ±5–9 rows per 300;
single-run records are noise until confirmed.
T able 2: Design laws for weight-space fact engineering, each derived from a measured failure documented
in the engineering ledger.
10 Conclusion
Facts can be written into a transformer the way an engineer writes to memory: measured, ad-
dressed, verified, and reversible. The write head is not gradient descent — it is a set of closed-form
constructions that speak the network’s own mechanism, discovered by dissecting what gradient de-
scent does when asked to cram. The resulting system beats its trained counterpart on recall while
refusing the damage trained methods accept as inevitable, dissolves the reversal curse by construc-
tion, and turns “what does the model know?” from a probing question into a lookup in a receipt.
The open frontier is conversational: making installed knowledge as reachable in free dialogue as
native knowledge is. The instruments, laws, and the attention-organ line of attack published here
are, we hope, enough for others to close it with us.
Acknowledgments. The engineering campaign behind this paper was conducted with substan-
tial assistance from Claude (Anthropic) as a research and engineering agent.
```

### Thinking

I'm looking at the SuperBake paper now and seeing a direct connection to our depth-timing findings—specifically how the g-hop finishes at layer 24 just as the h-hop consumes its input, creating that depth overlap we observed. Their composition mechanism in section 3.8 appears to address exactly this problem through their hop enrichment construction.

The key insight is that SuperBake places enrichment neurons early in the network (around layer 5-6, or 0.16× depth on Llama-8B), keying on the subject and injecting the answer entity's representation directly into the stream. This is fundamentally different from our approach of writing the country at layer 24—by that point, the h-hop window has already passed. SuperBake's early enrichment ensures the downstream native machinery has access to the required information from the start, following what they call the "ordering law": enrichment must be written before every key harvest.

The challenge for our landmark-to-capital setup is that we can't recognize the country early from the landmark prompt (it's only decodable at layer 24), yet we need to inject it early for the h-hop to work. SuperBake sidesteps this by recognizing the subject early and writing the answer-entity representation early, keyed on that subject. But we're trying to build something more general—not a lookup table of specific landmark-to-country facts, but a wire that generalizes across different landmarks.

The deeper insight from SuperBake is that the composition write must happen at the enrichment band (around 0.16× depth, layers 5-6), not at the late layer where the intermediate becomes decodable. To make the country routable by the native h-hop, I need to inject it at that early enrichment band rather than at layer 24 where it becomes recognizable.

The amplification difference is stark: writes at layer 24 only have 11 layers above to amplify the signal, while early deposits at layers 5-6 ride through 19-26 native nonlinear layers of amplification. This is the depth-timing finding as a design law—I wrote too late, and the amplification happens upstream. Additionally, the transport laws show that constructed payloads degrade quickly across blocks, and quiet directions attenuate around 30×, which explains why my weak reinject_landed signal (0.033) isn't surviving the journey.

The key insight is that construction can actually exceed the native composition rate—relations that don't naturally compose go from 2/12 without enrichment to 11/12 with it, surpassing the ~6/10 rate at which a 6.9B model composes its own facts silently. This means construction can install multi-hop composition better than the model's native ability, which challenges my earlier assumption that gradients are uniquely required.

The difference in SuperBake's approach is timing and target: they write early in the enrichment band and directly to the answer-entity's own representation rather than a hand-picked geometry. So for our wire, instead of recognizing the country late at L24, I should recognize the landmark early (it's a token present from layer 0, so early recognition is feasible) and write the country's own representation at the enrichment band around L6, letting the native h-hop compose it into the capital. But this shifts what I'm actually trying to recognize.

The core tension is that SuperBake installs specific facts and gets composition on them, while our wire test needs to generalize across held landmarks and countries. A per-landmark enrichment would just be a lookup that fails on held-out data and doesn't generalize—unless the model's native g-hop somehow provides the country mapping, but we're constructing the enrichment ourselves, so that won't work.

The key insight from SuperBake's approach is the timing: whatever enrichment we inject should go in at the enrichment band early (~0.16× depth), not at the recognition layer like our fast_plate/hhop did at L24.

But here's the fundamental problem—we can only *recognize* the country late in the network (L24), yet we need to *write* it early (around L6) for the native h-hop to compose it. On a single forward pass, we can't write something at L6 that we only learn at L24. This is the depth-timing wall: recognition happens late, the composition-write band is early, and causality prevents reconciling them in one pass. That's precisely why chain-of-thought or re-encoding is necessary—emit the country as a token, then on the next pass it's available at position 0, layer 0, ready to be used.

SuperBake sidesteps this entirely because they install the country directly at build time; they already know the fact, so there's no need to recognize it from the landmark during inference. Now I'm trying to understand where GTSM loss fits into this picture. GTSM appears to be a gradient trajectory loss—a search and training method that finds a more routing-faithful, ternarizable delta. The insight Michael seems to be pointing at is that GTSM loss, combined with the depth-timing finding and SuperBake's demonstration that composition *can* be constructed early, might offer a path forward.

The synthesis could work like this: SuperBake proves that early enrichment writes are the key to constructible composition, and our depth-timing finding independently confirms that the amplification needs to happen upstream. GTSM loss could then be used as a gradient search to find the wire that respects this depth-timing constraint—placing the g-hop enrichment at the early band where the native h-hop can consume it. Alternatively, we could use SuperBake's construction laws directly to build a wire rather than relying on per-fact lookup, though the remaining question is how to handle the recognition piece.

Now I need to clarify what GTSM actually is and how it works, then bring together the trajectory loss mechanism, the depth-timing finding, and SuperBake's laws into a coherent design.
